Cover · page 1

The Belgravian

March 1998

The Belgravian

The official gazette of Belgrave Harriers

Number 226

Cover photos.

Race Walking is alive and well in Belgrave Harriers. Our young men and women show every sign of re-establishing the club as force to be reckoned with thanks to the efforts of the 'Whorlow' camp from down South and the 'Graham' crowd from East Anglia.

Front page: It's seven years ago since a little shrimp from the South Coast arrived at Battersea Park to take part in the national Run for Africa. He saw the coloured photographs of top Belgrave athletes cut from the pages of this magazine and pinned on the notice board. He declared there and then that he was going to join the Bels. The intervening years have seen Nigel Whorlow grow to well over 6 feet and make a huge impact as one of Britain's top young walkers. Already an "age-group" international and still only 17, he has a tremendous future ahead of him. We take great pleasure from the fact that it was images from this magazine that set him on the road to success - now it's time for his own picture to go on the board!

Back page: Laura Fryer is also making a name for herself, winning races all around the country and ranking among the very top athletes in her event.

Both pictures taken at the Belgrave Open Seven Miles at Wimbledon on 22nd November by Alan Mead.

Nigel WhorlowLaura FryerAlan Mead

Notice · page 2

European Champion Clubs T&F Cup 1998

Great Britain was relegated to Group B in 1997. This time around Belgrave Harriers will be representing the Nation in attempt to gain promotion back to Group A. The competition will take place in Belgrade, Yugoslavia on Saturday 30th May. We will be up against the top clubs from Portugal, Israel, Greece, Turkey, Slovakia, Denmark and Germany.

We have reserved British Airways flights for £210 for any supporters who want to come along. Out: depart Gatwick flight BA2888 Fri. 29 May 10:15 arrive 14:05. Back: depart flight Mon. 31May, times to be advised. Details of hotel are awaited.

Anyone interested should contact Bill Laws [phone number withheld] (office) A deposit of £50 is required, cheques made payable to Belgrave Harriers - first come first served.

Bill Laws

Marshalls Wanted

Belgrave Harriers have been asked to supply 10 course marshalls for the 'Race For Life' at Battersea Park on Wednesday 3rd June 1998. If you are interested in helping and would like to earn a little money for the Club please contact John Jeffery (leave a message on 0181-946 6859) or see him at Battersea Park track.

This race was first held in 1994 and since then over 45,000 women have taken part and raised £2million for the Imperial Cancer Research Fund. If you want to take part in this women only 5k race - entry forms will be found at Belgrave Hall in due course or contact John as above.

John Jeffery

Editorial · page 3

Many, many thanks to those Belgravians who have donated sums of money towards this magazine's production. We can't name you all – and we know that some of you don't want public recognition – but without that little extra contributed alongside your subscription, the 344,315 characters making up 70,870 words in these pages would never have seen print. We mention "that little extra", and certainly every £1 helps, but some members have been very generous indeed.

It's been another marvellous year of athletics for Belgrave Harriers with the Track & Field squad carrying off the 1997 Gold Cup and British Athletics League Division One title. Among the Junior Men we have some truly amazing athletes who individually gained international honours with Dwain Chamber's European 100 metres win in World Junior Record time being the highlight. Combined as a team they contributed greatly towards the Club's promotion to the National Junior League Premier Division.

But we are tight for space in this magazine and have therefore regretfully had to omit the full details of our progress in the Under 20 Track & Field team competitions. Those areas are covered in depth in Belgrave's Youth In Action magazine but we must at least record in these pages that apart from the Junior men's promotion:

The McDonald's League Team (under 17 men, under 15 and under 13 boys) placed 6th of the sixteen teams in the Southern Premier League and thus just missed out on going to Birmingham for the Auxiliary National Final. The Thameside League saw our under 15 girls place 5th and the under 13s 7th, while both boys teams were 5th. If you want to know more about these activities then get in touch and while stocks last we will attempt to get a Youth In Action to you.

Now, for all the Senior action and for Road Running, Cross Country and Race Walking for all ages – read on!

As we have said, we are tight for space. As the originator of these pages also has a strong bias towards running of the cross country variety he now makes no apology at all for high-jacking the rest of the white space on this sheet for his own purposes.

We have just about come to the end of the second of two successful seasons on the country. We've slipped a little in the Reebok Surrey League – from 2nd in 1996-97 to this winter's 4th – but to be honest, that is not so important as the fact that we have solidly entrenched ourselves again in the top division. The South of Thames Cross Country Association victories were superb. It's most unusual for a club to carry off both titles in one season, the sort of thing that comes from a team on the way up, but the amazing thing is that we could do it again next winter if we put our minds to it. We do need to strengthen our middle order up a bit (health problems and injuries hit us here) but top guns Gary Staines and Kassa Tadesse have done us proud. Clive Gilby has fitted into the team very nicely while tough guy Jim Estall has had an astonishing few months.

Now for the big road relays. We may be missing a few faces for the 12-Stage events as the alternative attractions of warm weather training and the London Marathon kick in. However, it's about time that we avoided getting hung up about the Thurrock event and remembering that it is only the 'Southern' – the qualifying race for the National 12-Stage at Sutton Coldfield. We must treat it as just that and when success in the event comes along one day then all well and good. But some of our guys have never once run around Sutton Park and measured themselves up against the lads from clubs like Tipton and Bingley. Let's qualify and pencil in a good day out at Sutton Park on April 18th.

Dwain ChamberGary StainesKassa TadesseClive GilbyJim Estall

Column · page 4

From the Belgravian

25 Years Ago ...1973

IT IS WITH GEAT SORROW that we have to record the death of Alf. Harley, a man who worked tremendously hard for his Club and athletics in general and who in return was held by all who knew him in high regard. Whatever the event, Alf. Harley always seemed to be there and it is almost impossible to visualise Belgrave's future without his dignified presence.

SURREY C.C. LEAGUE DIVISION 1. With the coming of Aldershot, Farnham & District to the Surrey Cross Country League it was hoped that a little more excitement would be injected into what, for the past seven years, has become a bit of a procession. It certainly was!

In the 3rd match at Streatham Common we were beaten by a mere 8 points with Les Presland, running with even more vigour since his move from Surrey A.C. [to A,F&D], providing the individual winner.

So, we went into the final act of this desperately close struggle just 18 points up and with our grip on the trophy gradually being loosened. With Gerry North still suffering from tonsillitis, John Stow stuck in a train at Raynes Park and our veterans girding themselves for the following day's National Veterans' race, our chances became even slimmer. Over the Coulsdon course, still patchy with snow, the men from west Surrey romped home 30 points clear and gained an overall lead of 12 points. Final League Position. 1, Aldershot, Farnham & Dist. 863; 2, Belgrave H 875; 3, Hercules Wimbledon AC 1464; 4, South London H 1466; 5, Croydon H 1481; 6, Herne Hill H 1793; 7, Ranelagh H 1998. [Editor: Belgrave H have found themselves unable to win this competition ever since ...]

NATIONAL VET'S CHAMPIONS. February 18th 1973 - Perry Bar Park, Birmingham.

By winning the third National Championship for veteran runners on this cold and windy Sunday afternoon, Belgrave annexed the team trophy for the first time and furnished the individual champion also in the sprightly form of Laurie O'Hara.

Laurie didn't just win - he slaughtered the opposing runners. Belgrave didn't just win - they annihilated the opposing teams. It was that sort of day, Claret and Gold all the way. And it was really like the old days to hear the whistles of surprise at the presentation ceremony when our winning score of 29 points was announced. 1, L.O'Hara 32:35; 10, C.Shippen 34:30; 18, P.Newell 35:08. Teams: 1, Belgrave H 29; Springburn 87; 3, TVH 94.

THE CLUB have been pleased to accept the kind offer of Mrs. Churcher of the late Harry Churcher's medals and have agreed to hold them in trust during her life time, after which they will become the property of the Club.

THE END OF AN ERA ... Sandy Law closes up his shoe making business to move to Eastbourne. For many years athletes have gone to Sandy's shop in Revelstoke, Road Wimbledon for a pair of hand made to measure running shoes, possibly his most famous customer being Roger Bannister, the first man to run a mile in under four minutes. Unfortunately in today's world manufacturers like Sandy find it increasingly difficult to compete with the mass production of shoes by the giants of the sports shoe business.

and, 50 Years Ago ... 1947/48

THE SOUTHERN COUNTIES C.C. CHAMPIONSHIPS. ... were held at Aylesford on February 28th ... The senior race was over a 10 miles course and from the start Sydney Wooderson went in front followed by Vic Blowfield, with Ted Stimpson also well up. Len Herbert was coming through well and Frank Abberton and Tom Clinton were also well placed.

Albert Frost unfortunately had a bad time but Tom Carter was running well and eventually became our sixth scoring man with Les Stokell unluckily missing a winner's medal by two places. We also won the Odling-Smee Cup, awarded to the first Surrey club to finish a complete team of twelve. Our positions were as follows:- 3 V.S.Blowfield 56:46; 4 E.S.Stimpson 57:24; 7 L.W.Herbert 57:50; 10 F.J.Abberton 58:05; 15 T.P.Clinton 58:20; 36 C.T.Carter 60:01; 38 C.L.Stokell 60:05; 65 R.W.Tobin 61:37; 84 S.R.Eales 62:25; 90 A.E.Frost 62:50; 106 A.F.Mitchell 63:28; 132 G.M.Pearson 64:15. Team result: 1 Belgrave H., 75 points; 2 Blackheath H., 113.

THE ENGLISH C.C. CHAMPIONSHIPS. These were held in Graves Park, Sheffield, on March 13th, and being a fine day a very large crowd watched the racing.

Once again Sydney Wooderson was up the front but this time there was a small bunch of runners with him including our own Vic Blowfield. On the last lap Wooderson made his effort but Vic kept with him stride for stride and then about half-a-mile from the finish Vic made a gallant attempt to get away from his man and succeeded up to a point. The only snag was that there was further to go than Vic had thought and Wooderson came again to beat Vic up the finishing straight.

Len Herbert had run well throughout as had Ted Stimpson, now getting back to his pre-war form, and with Frank Abberton also making a last lap effort, and Tom Carter and Tommy Clinton coming along well we found to our satisfaction that we had beaten Sutton Harriers, the holders, to record our fourth National victory.

Les Stokell was once again a good 7th man and Albert Frost and Dick Tobin completed our team. Positions were: 2 V.S.Blowfield; 12 L.W.Herbert; 15 E.S.Stimpson; 27 F.J.Abberton; 41 C.T.Carter; 57 T.P.Clinton; 64 C.L.Stokell; 95 A.E.Frost; 132 R.W.Tobin. Team scores were: 1 Belgrave H., 154; 2 Sutton H., 174; 3 Blackheath H., 183.

In the Junior race Eddie Short was again our first man home in 7th position, and to gain such a high place in this race speaks well for Eddie's strength and determination. Mike Heard also ran well in finishing 40th and Arthur Bruce ran pluckily on a damaged foot to be 56th, with Eric Bishop 65, R.Garwood 112, L.C.Bishop 117, and L.A.Adams 138. In the team race we were placed 8th out of an entry of 30 clubs so can be well satisfied.

WITH THE WALKERS. And so to the National 20 at Sudbury. Our teams were supported by what must have been the biggest crowd of Club Members and friends ever seen at a road race. Our Road Captain Charlie Churcher surprised most people by not adopting his usual get-a-way method and the opening stages of the race were unusually slow. Later on, however, Charlie developed stomach trouble and was obliged to fall back. About the same time Colin Sutton had trouble with his shoes and lost a lot of ground. In the centre of the field, however, our Vice Captain, Jack Bidgood, was doing some excellent work holding together about half a dozen of our walkers which no doubt had much to do with the ultimate result of the race. On recovering from his trouble, Colin Sutton battled bravely on recovering position after position and eventually caught up on Jack Bidgood and Co. Charlie, however, had continued to drop back and with our old friends, Surrey W.C., holding two places in the first five, a win for us seemed out of the question. Eventually our lads finished in a procession with five consecutive placings from 18 to 22 and backed up by George Bentley, Joe Wilson and Joe Coleman, we succeeded in taking the Nijmegan Shield with our eight walkers in the first 48, an excellent performance on which all are to be congratulated.

WORLD'S RECORD FROM HARRY CHURCHER. And so to the most notable success in the Club's history for many years – our Track [Walking] Captain's achievement in setting up a new World's Record for 5 miles.

Alf. HarleyLes PreslandGerry NorthJohn StowLaurie O'HaraC.ShippenP.NewellMrs. ChurcherHarry ChurcherSandy LawRoger BannisterSydney WoodersonVic BlowfieldTed StimpsonLen HerbertFrank AbbertonTom ClintonAlbert FrostTom CarterLes StokellDick TobinS.R.EalesA.F.MitchellG.M.PearsonEddie ShortMike HeardArthur BruceEric BishopR.GarwoodL.C.BishopL.A.AdamsCharlie ChurcherColin SuttonJack BidgoodGeorge BentleyJoe WilsonJoe Coleman

Column · page 5

The President writes

Lionel Mann R.V.M.

I find that I am now about halfway through my presidential year and am amazed at how quickly the time has passed. It is a great honour to be elected to the office of President of our famous club and I much appreciate the fact.

During the past few months I have tried to attend as many committees and functions as possible. While attending committee meetings I have been pleased to see how many hard working and diligent committee members we have, working for the good and upward mobility of Belgrave Harriers. These several committees are of course run by their separate secretaries but so well held together by the Belgrave Committee Chairman (Alan Mead) that the club has a great sense of united direction. I believe that it is of paramount importance to our club that we do maintain this sense of union amongst our various athletic disciplines and club interests. It would be a poor reflection on Belgrave Harriers if we should be seen by people outside the club to be disunited within. As officers and members we should always be so aware that by working ever closer together whether in committee room or the competitors' arena, we can retain and even improve the greatness of Belgrave Harriers.

In this, my first opportunity of a "Presidential Address", I would like to thank immediate Past President Ted Pallant for the smooth efficiency of his handover to me, with all his helpful advice and support. Thanks also go particularly to all those other club stalwarts who have been my friends over so many years and especially over these past few months of my presidential term.

Highlights of my year so far have been, and I must of course start with, the proposal and election to President, quickly followed by a superb Club Dinner and the Youth Awards evening at the Polka Theatre – all this before Christmas. Since then we have enjoyed the increasingly popular Burns Supper which I am pleased to help organize with the ever-energetic Derek Manning and his team.

I was amazed at the quality and content of the Youth Scheme's magazine, a really superb production which can only complement the Belgravian; although the thought does cross my mind that they could both reach a wider audience if they were put together and perhaps produced twice a year. Food for thought perhaps.

In closing I would like to extend to all Belgravians everywhere my very best wishes for a successful 1998 season, whatever your particular discipline or standard of competition.

Lionel Mann R.V.M. President of Belgrave Harriers 1997-98

Lionel MannAlan MeadTed PallantDerek Manning

Feature · page 6

A Profile of The President

Gordon Biscoe

written by Gordon Biscoe

It was during the anniversary celebrations for Bill Lucas last April when it was realised that an almost complete London to Brighton relay team of yesteryear was present and that a reunion photograph would be a good idea. It fell to me to try and get them all into one place for a few moments, but when I tried to shepherd Lionel alongside the others I was told "But I never ran the Brighton – before my time!"

The point is that Lionel has become such a part of the 'Belgrave furniture', such an integrated member of the road and cross country scene that, in spite of all those years as secretary and team manager I could not reconcile as to him not having been in the team.

It was in fact 1965 when Lionel was spotted training at Battersea Park by our immediate Past President Ted Pallant and Joe Dooley *. They were told, when they approached him, that he was only training to keep fit for football. Always on the look out for potential talent,Ted's persuasive tongue went to work and Lionel was duly signed for Belgrave – and what a signing that turned out to be.

Never one to stand on ceremony or do things by halves, Lionel quickly made his mark in our teams, both on the track and the road, as well as over the country. No mean feat in itself considering the middle and long distance talent we had in those days. By 1967 he was 'runner up' in the Club 20 mile Road Championship and earned himself his first representative appearance for his native county Beds & Hunts.

So started an outstanding career in athletics with some of his achievements only just falling short of international recognition, as the recorded times below bear witness. Space does not allow a complete examination of his successes over the years but a brief summary, which must suffice, shows Lionel as having: –

◆ Competed on road, on the track and over the country at all distances from 400 metres to 37½ miles, as well as a number of hill races in Scotland – only small things mind you, like Ben Nevis at 4,406 feet in 2hrs 18m 33secs!

◆ Been Club Champion sixteen times (and runner up on another eighteen occasions) at 5 ¾ miles; 20 miles and Marathon on the road; 5km and 10km on the track and 7½ and 10 miles cross country.

◆ Been Bedford & Huntingdonshire County Champion four times over 5km, 10miles and 20 miles; Civil Service Champion at 5km and Marathon and Vets AC Champion three times at different age groups over 10 miles on the road.

◆ Represented the Civil Service, Kingston Borough and HMY Britannia on numerous occasions.

Lionel leads Bob Smith, Dick Piotrowski, and Adrian Major during the Belgrave 7½ miles Cross Country Championship of 1968 but all four had to give way to Tony Fairclough (1st), Laurie O'Hara (2nd) and Pat O'Connor (3rd) by the finish. The field was 36 strong.

◆ Achieved personal best times of:

◆ Has to date run around 1720 races and a total distance of approximately 85,000 miles – equivalent to 2½ times round the world – these figures subject to 'update' as he has not stopped yet!

Alongside this active career, which earned him a Club Honours Badge in 1968, he has combined official duties as well. Lionel was a committee member 1968/69; Road Running Captain 1969/76 and medals secretary 1987 to the present day. His club efforts were rewarded with election to Life Membership in 1989.

While all this was going on, Lionel still found time to marry his charming wife Pat, raise a family, (Robert, Katherine and James who have also turned out in Club Colours) and advance his career as a chef with the Royal Household. This he has done with great success, having now attained the position of Chief Chef at Buckingham Palace and the award of the Royal Victorian Medal.

Needless to say such a career has led to many unsociable hours and days but from the start Lionel was determined not to let the training suffer, so whenever he goes on Royal duties throughout the world, the kit goes too. In his more competitive days when alongside the likes of Pat O'Connor and Bill Weller they were the scourge of the road running scene, Lionel could often be spotted by the early riser, keeping the mileage up by running, cycling or even pushing the bike between his home in Molesey and Buckingham Palace!

As if all this was not enough he has for many years helped run a Scout Venture Group near his home and acted as a lynch pin for the Royal Household Sports and Social Club, organising many of their functions and occasionally returning to his first love by turning out for their football team. In latter years he has also been joining forces with Derek Manning in master-minding the Club's Burns Night suppers.

Lionel Mann is now known throughout the athletic fraternity, particularly 'on the road' where he was at his formidable best. During his time he has not only been an uncomplicated loyal member, but a great ambassador for Belgrave as well as for athletics in general; truly an athletes, athlete.

It was typical of Lionel when told of his nomination as our President for 1997/98 to comment, "well, if I am going to do the job I aim to do it properly". We could want for no better man for the task!

* Older members may remember Joe Dooley, referred to above, who regularly trained at Battersea and who for many years owned and wore that 'famous' Club vest which was a series of holes, held together by pieces of material in faded Belgrave colours!

Achieved personal best times of:
800m1:57.8
1 mile4:16

Column · page 7

Sidetracks

We were very concerned to hear that Pam Davies had been taken ill while running from Belgrave Hall one Saturday in late January. Pam was taken to St. George's Hospital where she was soon discharged. The following day, at home, she didn't seem too bad at first but as the day progressed she did become very unwell and eventually suffered a stroke. After a week in intensive care Pam was allowed out of bed and at the time of writing, at the end of February, she is undergoing physiotherapy. Still in hospital, steady progress is being made and all Belgravians will want to join in sending her best wishes for her journey down the road to full fitness and health. We look forward to seeing you back at the Hall again soon Pam.

The cross country crowd is growing. Recently we welcomed two week old Matthew Gilby to his first cross country race where his dad ran to 5th in the South of Thames Championship. Congratulations to Jodie and Clive on the safe arrival of their baby Matthew, a brother for Daniel.

Baby Thomas Staines made his entrance on Sunday 22nd February and weighed in at 7lbs 2oz. We hear that he's got a fair old pair of legs on him – but then with a mum and dad like Linda and Gary he would have, wouldn't he. Our very best wishes to the Staines family. We are very pleased for you.

"Suffolk" Mick Graham has recently passed the first stage of his Race Walking coaching examination; we hear that he is well on the way towards qualifying as a judge as well. All this alongside finding himself, in the space of a couple of seasons, promoted from cross country "also ran" to county champion walker. Yes – he picked up the Suffolk 10 mile title to go with his track 3k title a few weeks ago. Who says you can't teach an old dog new tricks!

Dave McMillan is training well. The last time we heard, he was regularly up to 10 miles on a Saturday afternoon in his quest to return to the London Marathon field. He recently completed the Hogs Back race and a small celebration was held afterwards to salute his progress. Some might not have walked again after those awful injuries. Incidentally, the paramedic who saved his life at the roadside received an award for his marvellous work.

We must record our thanks to Mervyn Dessa for many years work as a track judge. Mervyn, who has not been keeping too well of late, has decided to retire from judging this summer and will be sorely missed. He tells us that he has been with the Club for seven years during which time he has greatly enjoyed our company and friendship.

Win Shepherd is another stalwart at our track meetings, churning out the result sheets in the "caravan". Win is troubled badly by arthritis in her hands which makes recording the results very difficult. She would very much like to pass on her expertise to someone else. Any offers? Many thanks to you also Win – how many points you totted up for us over the years?

Yet again Bill Couzens carried off the Denmark Pot for the Darts Championship. It was Bill's eleventh win in the 34 years that the event has been held. Next nearest is John Martin on five wins.

More sidetracks on page 9

Sidetracks

Eagle-eyed Pete Mulholland spotted a little howler in our last issue. On page 59 we showed Jim Estall runing past the Peace Pagoda during a VAC 5.2-miler and mentioned that the edifice had only seen one faster run by a veteran – and that by Laurie O'Hara. Knowing that we are sticklers for accuracy, Pete says, "I would point out that Laurie's 26:26 for the Veterans 5.2 in Battersea Park was set in 1976 – before the Peace Pagoda was built!" It's good to know that every word of our magazine is devoured by those outside our club as well!

Another non-Belgravian to read our mag., Dave Cocksedge, points out a couple of adjustments that should be made to our 1996 T&F Rankings. Phil Goedluck's 10.88 100m was windy (+3.3) while we failed to record that Jon Ridgeon had a 47.7 400m at Barn Elms on 8 Sep and Ben Cascoe a 52.22m javelin throw at Croydon on 8 Jun. Thanks Dave.

Deniz Gezgin, our popular young 400 metre runner, returned to his home country of Turkey for the summer and at Ankara ran in Junior European Clubs Cup 200 metres where he clocked 21.8. Not one to let grass grow under his feet he is now at Arkansas University but is keeping in touch via coach Bob Bridges.

Janine Brown from Folkestone became an outstanding young middle-distance runner during her time with us. She is now competing for her local club Ashford AC and we were delighted to see her representing them in the SMC National Junior League promotion match. She sends her best wishes to all those in the Club who knew her.

Not surprisingly Dwain Chambers received plenty of accolades after his success in retaining the European Junior 100m Championship in World Junior Record time. Among his awards were the George Hogflesh Trophy as the top English T&F Junior of 1997 (World Junior 100m record 10.06) and the Joe Turner Memorial Trophy for best male U20 AAA Champion (100m in 10.32).

Seen training from Belgrave Hall: Hendrick Ramaala, Republic of South Africa 10k record holder and winner of the Great North Run ending a run of six Kenyan victories. Pity we can't turn him out for the "12-Stage".

We hear that Ron Severn has regretfully had to step down as organizer of our Annual Club Dinner. We thank you greatly Ron for your efforts in making sure that this event has continued to be among the highlights of our club year. Good news is that someone has already stepped in to take up the reins – our ever energetic Hon. Treasurer Derek Manning. Derek informs us that the 1998 Dinner will again be held at The Preston Cross Hotel, on Saturday 14th November. Ticket prices remain unchanged at £27.

Jacqui Smiter has had a short spell in hospital for a wrist operation. We hope that her recovery is swift and look forward to her teaming up with Cynthia James again this summer to capitalize on our promotion the the Women's Southern League Division Two.

Arthur Penny, Empire Games 6 miles Champion in 1934, recently reached his 90th birthday and is just coming up to 70 years of continuous club membership. Arthur underwent an operation for gallstones recently but we hear that he keeping well and we are looking forward to a little get-together at the Hall soon when we can celebrate his long and illustrious time as a Belgrave Harrier.

Talking of birthdays – here's a few that might stun you: Charlie Manning, Charlie Walker, Arthur Bruce and Reg Hopkins all reach their three-score and ten in 1998. Just think, a whole new age-group in which to record Club Best Performances!

Does anyone know the whereabouts of Frank Ward, our Life Member and ex-Hon. Treasurer? Please contact the Membership Secretary Carl Lawton [phone number withheld] if you have Frank's 'phone number or address.

Steve Williams writes from deepest Sussex. A true statistician, the article about the Christmas Paalauf in our last issue prompted him to inform us that he has now, on his own, achieved the combined ages of John Gladwin and himself when they won the event (and it seems like yesterday).

Having taken early retirement, Steve says that the days and weeks run into one another at a pace never experienced when getting on the Northern Line five days a week! In spite of being surrounded by superb downlands and cliffs, he no longer runs or jogs due to a hip problem; his heart and mind are willing but his skeleton isn't. Nevertheless, he and Sue get in plenty of walks, especially now that they have re-housed a 5 year-old Labrador/Golden Retriever cross.

Work now seems an even dirtier word than ever it was and although they live their lives along principles established by Mr. Micawber, Steve says that there's a smug look on his face when he reflects on former colleagues now chained to desks in Coventry when, on a sunny afternoon, he sups his half of Sussex bitter at the Golden Galleon while overlooking the meandering Cuckmere River.

Steve and Sue send their best wishes to all.

A member of our veterans' T&F team in 1996 was Ian Vaughan-Arbuckle, a member of Ryston Runners but second-claim Belgrave due to the fact that he lived and worked in London during the week. Unfortunately our old friend "Von Carbunkle" has been forced to quit running due to persistant knee problems.

At a southern league match last summer we ran into old second-claim Belgravian Peter Hall, now a stalwart (and field event referee) with the host club on that day, Milton Keynes.

It wasn't long before tales of the "old days" were being recalled. As a Polytechnic Harrier he was a keen supporter of the London–Brighton relay where Belgrave, Poly. and other rival clubs met up at the Black Swan at Pease Pottage on the way back to town. There they would attempt to out-sing each other with ever more doubtful ditties. One year even the coach driver got so drunk that a Poly member with an HGV licence had to drive the Poly bus back home.

Peter passes on his best wishes to old training mates, particularly those from the Hurlingham days: Fred Paget, Lionel Mann, Pat Newell and Chas. Walker.

Too late for the Winter Fixture List we received the names of the Captains of our young women's winter teams: Under 20 Women – Debbie Hearn and Under 15 Girls – Rebecca Lester.

We have recently heard from John Thresher a regular first team man on track and country in the '60s who has been living in Toronto, Ontario for many years now. John has remained very much involved with the sport of athletics and has just accepted the position of President and Chief Executive Officer of Athletics Canada. What a hot seat to sit in. John will be establishing a new executive office for Athletics Canada in Toronto but the national office will remain in Ottawa for the time being so he'll have to get used to a fair bit of commuting. And then there's the Commonwealth Games in Kuala Lumpur, the "Worlds" in Seville, the Olympics in Sydney – tough eh! John is still running four times a week but only about four miles at a time.

Our Life Member Doris Nice tells us that she enjoys reading the Belgravian, particularly the bits and pieces about the older members she has known through many years in the sport. When she read the feature on our immediate Past President Ted Pallant she felt that it was like reading her own history. "I always thought I knew the name when I saw it on the fixture cards," she writes, "but now I can see that Ted and I went to the same schools." Doris was born in Battersea and trained regularly for athletics in Battersea Park but as Belgrave didn't have a ladies section in the '50s, joined Surrey AC. Then, as a respected member of Surrey AC she automatically became a Life Member of Belgrave when both clubs amalgamated.

Report · page 8

Hon. Secretary's Report to the 1997 Annual General Meeting

C. Henn

Sadly, three long-standing members of the Club died during the year: Lovell Cole, Jack Goswell (President 1964/65) and Laurie O'Hara.

One matter of Club administration: the Committee have approved a procedure for handling any future disciplinary case. It includes among other things the right to appeal to a panel made up of Committee members who took no part in the original disciplinary hearing; it will be for this panel to decide in the light of the written appeal whether there are grounds for an actual appeal hearing. I will provide a copy of the statement of procedure for any member who wishes.

In terms of athletic competition, the past year has overall been possibly our most successful ever.

I will start at senior level. Our men have had yet another extremely successful track and field season. Under the team management of John Jeffery and Bill Laws they won both the First Division of the British League and the British League Cup by big margins. Despite the difficulties caused by the British League team always having first claim on athletes, the men's Southern League team managed by Bob Bridges have maintained their membership of Division 2 to which they were promoted last year. In the Rosenheim League, with Pat Mead as team manager, we won all but one match in our division and went on for the fourth successive year to win the league final. Our women's Southern League team, managed by Jacqui Smiter and Cynthia James, have subject to confirmation achieved their aim of gaining promotion to Division 2; and Sarah Roberts (400m) became our first female T&F international for a good few years.

Seven first-claim members of the Club were selected for the World Championships in Athens: Darren Campbell (100m); both he and Dwain Chambers (still a junior) in the 4x100m relay, for which they received bronze medals; Chris Rawlinson (400m hurdles); Brendan Reilly (HJ); Dave Smith (hammer); Andy Tulloch (110m hurdles); and Matt Yates (1500m). Add to that Adrian Passey, second-claim, in the 5000m. And Geoff Dearman (4x400m), Matt Douglas (400m hurdles) and Stuart Faben (javelin) gained selection for the European U23 championships.

Last year's very welcome improving trend over the country has continued. Our senior men, managed by Alan Mead, were second in Division 1 of the Surrey League, and indeed won the last match. We were second team in the South of the Thames Senior, and became Surrey team champions for the first time since 1967, with Gary Staines winning the individual race. The women, managed by Harry Porter, did enough to remain in Division 1 of their Surrey League. On the road, Belgrave won the men's team race in last September's Great North Run; and Paul Evans went on to win the Chicago Marathon in a very fine 2:08:52 (with Gary Staines fifth) and to return 2:09:18 in the London Marathon in April.

I turn to the younger age groups. What we hope will become the highlight of the season for our younger teams is still to come – at Copthall Stadium on Sunday 21st September, in the promotion match in the National Junior League. Finishing in the top two [in the event they were first!] would see the Belgrave U20 men's team, which has been managed by John Powell, into the Premier Division for the first time ever. The young athletes team, now managed by Laurence Rogers, fell marginally short of equalling last year's feat of reaching the McDonald's Young Athletes League national finals, but remain one of the top sides in the country.

Individually the highlight of the season at junior level was Dwain Chambers's gold medal in the European Junior Championships, in the world U20 record time of 10.06 seconds. (You can see Dwain in action at Copthall on Sunday, where he has agreed to run 100m, 200m and the relay for the Club.) Three other Belgrave Harriers were selected for these championships: Philips Idowu, who narrowly missed a medal when fourth in the triple jump, Michael Parper (400m) and James Hilston (4x400m). Other very fine performances during the season included Michael Parper's win in the Surrey County senior 400m, while still a junior, in a championship best time; and Philips Idowu's championship best triple jump at the English Schools' meeting. Marlon Kerr and Chris Lambert also gained selection for England in the schools international meeting.

Our young walkers, from the youngest age-groups through to the U20s, have also gone from strength to strength under the management of Steve Whorlow, and latterly also Mabon Dane. Times have been set that have not been seen since the '60s and '70s, and international honours have been awarded to several, spearheaded by Nigel Whorlow.

The Schoolslink project has again been a great success, with Belgrave coaches visiting many local schools, and our relationship with the local authority in Wandsworth has been further cemented with a second year of the borough's schools track and field competition. A schools liaison officer has now been appointed.

After this story of success I need, I am afraid, to end by repeating the concluding remarks of my report at the last AGM. More help is needed in the work that it takes to operate the Club and to provide the services – of all kinds – that members expect. All too few people are doing the jobs that need doing – on the Committee, as coaches, managing teams, or officiating at meetings. Several are doubling up on jobs in the Club, and frequently they are working full time to earn a living as well.

As you can see from the agenda, a number of Committee posts remain vacant.

We are beginning to be recognised as a club that never fails to turn out its quota of officials, and a good number of them are making the effort to reach higher graded status; but we take part in so many fixtures that we have to draw very deeply – too deeply – on the time of our most dedicated men and women, one or two of whom have judged at over 40 club matches. We badly need more officials, especially those prepared to travel to away meetings.

Moreover, some of those coaching and team managing – particularly among the younger age-groups – are extremely hard-pressed. I gather very worryingly that nobody is prepared to manage our young male cross-country teams in the season that is shortly to begin. [In fact Reg. Hopkins stepped in to fill the breach.] In any of these areas, if you think you can help, or even know someone who might, please let the appropriate person know. Anyone who can spare the time to help in any of these roles will be very welcome.

C. Henn 18 September 1997.

Feature · page 8

Belgrave vs. Birchfield 1935

Continuing his quest for items of sporting historial interest Eric Hall recently purchased a programme for the British Games of 10th June 1935.

There was a 3 Mile Match between Belgrave and Birchfield and the introduction states that Belgrave "prior to the Great War did little ....since, however, their rise to fame has been remarkable" . It then lists the various wins both cross country and walking. The Captain that day was Bert Footer and the rest of the team were Allum, Carter, Flower, Ginty, Parker, Patience; Shaw, Ring, Rice, Shields and Taylor. The June/July 1935 Belgravian reports that "the race will be long remembered by those who had the pleasure to witness it. At 1 mile Birchfield led; at 2 miles we were leading handsomely, but in the last lap our Midland friends managed to pack in together and inflict a 4 points defeat upon us." Jim Ginty won the event with a time of 14 mins 43 secs.

Another item in the programme took my eye and seems quite relevant even today!

An Open Flash Relay provided a "novel competition....and ideal practice for fast passing of the baton. Errors in this respect have more than once put us out of Olympic and International events". The race was for teams of three with a run up of 40 yards; exchange 15 yards; run 40; exchange 15 and run in 40 yards. A Belgrave team of Dyke, Bone and Mewes was entered. Evidently it was a regular race as previous times of 10.8 etc are given and winners had been Poly and Blackheath. It does seem, however, that although it was well intentioned the lessons on baton changing need to be revived! The Surrey AC team included a G. H. G. Dyson who became the national coach post war and was then responsible for the relay teams who still, occasionally, made a mess of things.

Eric HallBert FooterAllumCarterFlowerParkerPatienceShawRingRiceShieldsTaylorJim GintyDykeBoneMewesG. H. G. Dyson

Report · page 10

George's 50th Marathon Achieved

As previously reported, George Piddington, a member of the Bels. for nigh-on 40 years, suffered a heart attack while out running and training for that elusive 50th marathon. Left with Parkinson's disease one might have been forgiven for thinking – "Well that's it, he'll never do it now". Never underestimate a marathoner, however, and also, never underestimate the friends one makes in this great sport of ours. Living on Hayling Island since 1965, George has also been with Havant AC for the last twenty-seven years and nine of his gallant colleagues from that club rallied round and took turns to push him around the South Coast Marathon, collecting for the Parkinson's Disease Society along the way.

In his NHS Mk I rear-engined wheelchair, George clocked 3 hrs. 31 mins., eight minutes short of his pb, with his team of "pushers" truly delighted to take on three mile stints each to help him complete his 50th.

"He has done so much to help athletes at the club over the years and it was a great way of saying thankyou to him."

The run was not without incident. At one changeover point the support car's doors became locked with the car keys on the inside and the support team on the outside. As a "pusher" and George sped off into the distance the next leg runner was left behind – with transport but unable to get inside it. After some quick thinking she raced off after George on a borrowed bike, leaving behind a panicking support crew.

The RAC were summoned but couldn't promise to arrive for three quarters of an hour. An Irish runner, stopping to unwrap a boiled sweet, offered some advice, and after poking about with an old coat hanger, miraculously sprang all the car door buttons. Faster than one could blink (or remove the coat hanger) the car was full and being driven off in hot pursuit.

The rest of the changeovers went like clockwork and the team, consisting of George and all nine of his club-mates, crossed the finishing line together – with George just a fraction ahead. You know, it didn't seem to matter how hard they ran, old George always seemed to maintain his position in front!

Adapted from an article in Havant AC's Track Record

George Piddington

Obituary · page 10

Gwen Couzens

Very sadly we must announce that Gwen Couzens passed away before Christmas after a prolonged and courageous battle with illness.

Like many another lady who married a member of our club, she immediately became a "Belgravian", long before our doors were officially officially opened to female members. Looking back over the years we can see her helping our club in so many ways. She was there lending a hand when the jumble sales were one of our methods of raising additional cash. Social functions just did not seem to take place without Gwen's cheerful presence. She supported Bill through his athletic days in the Southern League, hosted those marvellous New Year parties, saw both her children become members of the Club and was bound up with the fortunes of the Bels. as we rose through leagues.

A true sign of our regard for Gwen was that the chapel at Lower Morden was packed with family, friends and club members, many of those present falling into more than one of those categories. We will all miss her and we offer our deepest sympathy to her family, particularly Bill, Tina and David.

Bill Couzens writes:

To my fellow Belgravians and friends,

Thank you for the many cards and letters of sympathy and the lovely floral and written tributes in memory of my dear wife Gwen.

This has been a great comfort to myself and my family at this sad time. Your kindness is deeply appreciated.

Gwen CouzensBill CouzensTinaDavid

Letter · page 10

Clive Shippen writes from Sydney

How time flies: we have to remind ouselves that it will soon be two years since he left these shores. Clive's business is now off the gound and he is a Fellow of the Institute of Engineers Australia. Still jogging three times a week, he also finds time to assist in pole vault coaching at Homebush track where he advises Peter Banks who won the Aussie decathlon title with 7915 points. Peter is being tipped to reach 8500 by Sydney 2000. Another lad aided by Clive also has outstanding potential – a 6'2" 17 year-old whose talent has already taken him to a 49.50m discus throw, an 11.05 100m, 6.80m long jump and 17.2 high hurdles. Note the name – Peter Elvy.

Clive tells us of an old Belgravian that he ran into:

' I attended Homebush recently for a pole vault session with Peter. I was wearing my Belgrave track-suit, cap and carrying a Belgrave bag (how ostentatious!). An old grey-haired gentleman, in the process of coaching a girl vaulter, spotted my track-suit, approached me and said, "What's Belgrave doing out here then?" I looked baffled. "Jim McGrath is the name," he said. "You probably won't know me but I used to belong to Belgrave before emigrating in 1961." It took a while for the penny to drop and for my brain cogs to turn back to that era, and then I remembered. Impossible to recognize him of course, but I well remember how invaluable he was to us in trophy meetings with his decathlon skills. '

Jim featured well up in our ranking lists for 1959 in every field event except the triple jump and hammer and was also our best high hurdler for the year. By 1960 he was our top high jumper, long jumper, discus thrower, pole vaulter – and need we say – decathlete.

Clive ShippenPeter BanksPeter ElvyJim McGrath

Obituary · page 10

We also regret to record that two other Belgravians have passed on recently.

Len Adams had not kept up his membership in recent years but was a regular runner with us in the 'fifties and 'sixties and was a good friend of the late Tom Carter. He must have always kept Belgrave close to his heart for his last wishes were that "... there should be no flowers but donations to be made to the Belgrave Harriers". Leo Coy kindly represented us at his funeral in Dartford.

Tony Whincup died in October aged 75. We hadn't seen a lot of Tony in recent years but, a Belgravian since 1948, he was one of those myriad members who have remained true to his club over a long period. Like many others he got little back for his annual subscription other than the pleasure of knowing that his contribution went towards keeping the club moving forward.

Obituary · page 11

LAURIE O'HARA (1932-1997)

Clive Shippen

It is with much sadness that Belgrave Harriers announce the death of Laurie O'Hara at the age of 65 years. He died only the day after attending a social function at Belgrave Headquarters.

Laurie was probably the most consistent middle-distance veteran runner during the period 1972-75 when he recorded personal bests of 3:58.5 (1500m), 8:22.0 (3000m) and 14:28.2 (5000m). In 1973 he won the British Veterans National Cross Country title and led Belgrave to the team title in 1973 and 1974; it was in that latter year that he topped the World rankings at 3,000 metres and 5,000 metres.

He was a late starter. During his twenties he played football for Harrow, Leatherhead and then, on amateur forms, for Reading FC. He kept fit during the summers by running at Hurlingham track and it was there that a Belgrave training pack discovered just how good he was at interval work. Laurie was a natural at such training and soon abandoned football to take up athletics at the late age of 29 years. Within weeks he was competing for the first team and after only a few months he recorded a fastest stage time in the London to Brighton Relay, gaining a team gold into the bargain. For the next ten years he was a familiar figure up and down the country; running through each year's fixture list with gay abandon, making up for his late start. Laurie reached veteran status in 1972, just as the World Veteran Association was formed. The inaugural international meetings at Crystal Palace and Cologne brought together for the first time teams from Canada, USA and Australia, sparking interest across Europe.

Laurie made his mark on the International scene swiftly by scoring fine wins in the 5,000 metres events at both venues.

Housed inside that diminutive 5 foot 4 inches, 8 stone frame of his was a ferocious finishing kick. Many of his winning margins were achieved in the last 150 metres and for this he gained quite a reputation. Medals continued to come his way as he moved up through the age categories but a persistent virus problem never gave him the chance to tackle the M65 group. Laurie was a quiet unassuming man, well respected and much liked. He will be sadly missed by his club and the veteran fraternity in general. Belgrave Harriers extend their condolences to his wife and family.

Clive Shippen

We will always remember Laurie O'Hara as a winner.

The Dysart Dash 10k at Petersham on June 30th 1996 was one of Laurie's very last races and yet again he was the first man home in his category.

Laurie O'HaraClive ShippenRon LinsteadRay O'Donoghue
See it on the printed page →
Feature · page 11

Memories of Laurie ...

TVH Road Relay, Cranford, mid '60s. One hears runners talking of 'flying' when they are running at their best. Whilst among the runners milling about near takeover area I looked up and saw the leaders come into sight over the bridge. The sun was low in the sky and there was Laurie's distinctive and diminutive figure, silhouetted for a brief moment as he crested the rise. He was in full flow and I would swear that not once did his shoes touch the ground as he headed for home. A full twelve inches of golden sunlight shone between his shoes and the tarmac. Laurie was flying! (CEM)

Southern Road Relay, London – Brighton, 1963. Portsmouth AC with their international runners were the team in those days. In the Southern Road Relay Laurie was on the 7th stage and took the baton from a youthful Tony Fairclough who had run his socks off to move up to within ten seconds of the leader. Laurie set off like a man with a mission and as the Gatwick Airport stretch was reached, came up to the Pompey runner's shoulder. The whole thing was recorded by a Belgrave movie camera team on a motor-bike. As the two runners approached a short underpass our man's last action before the two runner disappeared for an instant into the shadows was to turn his head and cheekily wink at the camera crew. Ten seconds later and out into the sunlight again – Laurie was 5 yards ahead and going away. Belgrave went on to win the Southern Road Relay that day to interrupt Portsmouth's domination of the event. (ARM)

LaurieTony Fairclough

Obituary · page 12

JACK GOSWELL, R.I.P.

John Keown

Just before Easter, Jack Goswell, Past President and Life Member of Belgrave Harriers, died after a short period in hospital.

Jack was a great Belgravian. He had two driving ambitions; to enhance the name of Belgrave and improve the organisation of the Race Walking Association Championships. Although not a top competitor, he was one of the all-time greats of race walking. He did take part in a few club races and the Nijmegan Marches.

Jack joined Belgrave Harriers in April 1951 and within 17 months became the Hon. Race Walking Secretary. But he was much more than a Secretary, being coach, adviser and a brilliant team manager.

Around a nucleus of champions like Stan Vickers, Eric Hall, Ray Middleton and Ray Hall, he built teams which, during his 15 years as Walking Secretary finished first on 18 occasions, second 13 times and third on seven occasions for Belgrave Harriers in Senior R.W.A. national road walking championships. In 1957 the club, under his guidance, took first place in all the R.W.A. championships in one year; 10 miles at Coventry, 20 miles at Hendon and 50k at Leyland, a feat never achieved before by any club. The 20k championship was not added to the championship programme until 1965.

One can only marvel at the drive and enthusiasm which formed those teams and those which, on eight successive years between 1955 and 1962, won the Nijmegan Shield awarded then to the first club finishing a full team of eight walkers in the National 20 miles. Belgrave's and Jack's success was incredible when you remember that every walker who started must finish and none could afford to incur a judge's displeasure as each judge in a national championship then had the power to disqualify a competitor. Today the team size has been reduced to 6 and it takes 3 judges to disqualify a competitor.

Jack's success was due to his in depth knowledge of his walkers and the opposition, an ability to generate intense team spirit, absolute faith in his team selections, enough determination to drive exhausted walkers to give 101% and to take full advantage of the rules of competition. In those days feeding stations were far apart so competitors had to depend on help from club supporters or on the generosity of others to survive. Today national championships are held on multi-lap courses with fixed feeding stations every 5kms.

The Milan Trophy was awarded for the first time in 1962 to the club finishing the first full team of eight walkers in the R.W.A. 50k Championship. Belgrave, spearheaded by Ray Middleton, 2nd and Ray Hall, 3rd and marshalled by Jack, took the trophy.

Another job performed by Jack, with the help of his wife Joan, was organising the annual Belgrave Open 7 Miles Road Walk each November at Wimbledon. He undertook this task for over 25 years.

He was President of Belgrave Harriers in 1964–65 and for many years organiser of the '200' Club which helped to ensure that the club's finances were kept on a sound footing.

In December, 1953 Jack was elected to the R.W.A. (Southern Area) Committee. He became Chairman in 1961–2 and President in 1963–4.

On the death of Alf McSweeney (Highgate H) Jack became R.W.A. Hon. Championships Secretary in September, 1962. His experience of organising the Belgrave Open '7' and various other road walking races in the South, coupled with the invaluable support of his wife Joan, enabled him to carry out the duties of Hon. Championships Secretary with distinction. He held the post until the R.W.A. A.G.M. in September, 1967.

For over 20 years Jack and Joan compiled the results of all 'nationals' and produced on-the-spot results for distribution to everyone free of charge.

In 1971–72 Jack became R.W.A. National President and in September, 1973 he was made a R.W.A Life Member for long exceptional and meritous service to the Association. His wife Joan received the same honour a few years later.

Jack was a perfectionist, which meant some people found he was not easy to work with when producing results. Finishing lists (numbers and times) had to be checked and re-checked. He expected all names, numbers and times to be accurate, no matter what the weather conditions on race day. He expected everyone helping to compile results, including Joan (usually the 'typist), to be constantly alert. As I was frequently one of those involved I saw Jack's annoyance when some mistake caused a hold-up in the production of results. I discovered that Joan was a lady of infinite patience.

Over a period of some 25 years I had the privilege of serving on the R.W.A. Southern and National committees and various sub-committees with Jack and found him to be a very hard worker with a wide knowledge of current and past walkers and personalities in athletics. I think that he was disappointed that his abilities as a team manager were not put to the test as a G.B. walking team manager.

Unfortunately during the last few years constant leg trouble made it difficult for Jack who was a Grade I walking judge to attend races. This, combined with the fact that he was a very independent character who liked to keep his personal life private meant that he had very little contact with the race walking scene in recent years. I, like many of those who attended his funeral, was surprised to learn that Jack had a twin brother Tom. Our condolences to Tom and his family.

I shall always remember Jack as a great Belgravian, a man of great integrity who made sure that any task which he undertook for the Club or athletics was tackled with great drive and enthusiasm and completed satisfactorily.

May his soul rest in peace.

John Keown

Jack GoswellJoanStan VickersEric HallRay MiddletonRay HallAlf McSweeneyTomJohn KeownL. A. Metson & Son

Feature · page 13

. . . he was "Mr. Belgrave"

Eric Hall

For those of us who were lucky enough to be members of Belgrave during the period when Jack was Race Walking Secretary he was the man we looked to for everything to do with our sport - he was Mr. Belgrave so far as we were concerned.

Recollections flood in and they cover the whole range of sporting activity and emotions. He took over as Race Walking Secretary at a time when our fortunes were, comparatively, low. We had great teams pre and post war but by the early fifties many had retired. Jack's arrival on the scene, firstly as a competitor in 1951, and then only a short while later as Secretary changed all that. It is easier to give reasons for the change than it is to point to a date when things changed. Jack was first and foremost a motivator, a man you could not let down. Normally quiet encouragement was all he required but if need be he could use the whip to good effect. Jack always knew instinctively just what I needed, and I am sure I speak for many others. He was the team manager and unofficial coach who persuaded us to apply discipline to our training methods.

Tuesday evenings, in the winter, almost without exception, found him at Wimbledon. He counted us out and waited patiently for us all to return! A quick cup of tea and a rush down hill for the train home. In the summer he watched us on the track while the longer distance men did "the bridges". Once home he had to reconcile his little black book with the money he had collected for race entries, outings, the Race Walking Record or the Club football competition. We would often hear of how late he, and Joan, worked to ensure that all ran smoothly.

Race days saw him arrive early to organise our teams and, if it was a home fixture, sort out the officials and recording in readiness for the printing of the results and the prize giving. He took great pride not only in Belgrave's sporting successes but also in maintaining a very high standard of race promotion. He was more than ably supported by his wife, Joan, who was often to be found in cold, uncomfortable, venues ever ready with the typewriter and duplicator to ensure we all had a fully completed result sheet before we left for home. The pair of them were also a very formidable Belgrave team. Naturally enough both were pressed into service by the RWA, and other bodies, and so they became a corner stone of race walking at many venues.

An all day stroll with Jack was an entertainment especially if Harry Shields was in the party. No matter how short of breath, or how tired the legs, one or other was ready with a quick remark which not only made it all worthwhile but also took our mind off what later became known as "the pain barrier" ! we were not averse to playing tricks and I will always remember the day when Jack, this time on his faithful bicycle, rode ahead of us to the next cross roads to take a rest. We found a short cut across a field which meant that we avoided the cross roads and it was some while before we heard Jack's voice some 400 yards behind us telling us exactly what he thought of us! As he was carrying our lunches he would have had the last laugh anyway. It was not unusual for one or other of us to 'phone him on a Thursday to talk about the Saturday race and just before putting the receiver down we would assure him that we would be at Waterloo in good time for the 8.30am to Salisbury – the race being in Sheffield! Only Joan knew just what he had to put up with from an unruly pack of athletes all intent on having a good time – Jack it was though who added the necessary ingredient to ensure that success came our way.

During a race he would inform you of progress if he thought you needed to know or could do something about improving the position. For some of the time I was Club Captain which was a sinecure and Jack never once gave me bad news – always encouragement, always persuasion but never ever a moan.

He led us to great heights during the period from 1955 to 1965 and I was very fortunate to be a member of many of those teams and please bear in mind that, in those days, Belgrave turned out nearly 60 walkers – rather more than many total fields these days. Jack organised the teams and had in mind handicap prizes as well as National titles. We had more than our share of both and to us went the glory, the trips overseas and the prizes, but Jack shared in our satisfaction and fulfilled the prophesy made by Alf Harley, when he took the job on, that in a year or so he could have a great team! Given that you could wear a British vest and not make the Belgrave "A" team it was a fair forecast but if anything pleased Jack most of all I like to think it was the long sequence of wins in the Nijmegen Shield given for the first team to get all eight men home in the National 20 mile walk – Belgrave won it eight years in a row! He was also very proud of the National Championships he put on at Wimbledon which meant a considerable amount of work – including course measuring involving sweeping snow off the road in order to paint white mile marks!

The only occasions when Jack was absent from an event was if it clashed with an international at Twickenham – somehow he managed to get tickets every time but would never reveal the source.

Well Jack, as I have said in the past, Many Thanks for just being there and doing so much for so many of us. I am confident that you will soon be organising the recording angels into some sort of order with the aim to beat everyone else in getting the celestial handicap results out!

You could get contributions from many people and mount an issue of The Belgravian just dedicated to him – I have enough memories to fill a small book myself.

Eric Hall

JackJoanHarry ShieldsAlf HarleyPatsy LoganEric HallLen Thorpe

Report · page 14

The light at the end of the tunnel is now dazzling

Stephen Whorlow

Race Walking

The light at the end of the tunnel is now dazzling

Two years ago I said there was a light at the end of the Club's Race Walking "tunnel". That light is now shining bright and the Club has regained its status as a major force in the event. The Young Athletes Walking section has played a large part in this renaissance. They have joined the other sections of the Club in making the Bels one of the most successful all-round athletic clubs in the U.K.

1997 was expected to be fairly quite for the club after one or two of our teenage walkers had found life a bit more interesting elsewhere but it didn't turn out that way. The youngsters have gone from strength to strength, bringing back the spoils of competition time after time. It has been a busy year, with visits to the Midlands and the North being a regular occurrence. Sheffield was the venue for a Grand Prix, the National Road Walking Championships and the Inter-Area Match.

The Club has produced county road & track champions, regional road champions and national champions and medallists. We are now gaining rewards throughout the country and with others coming in at the lower age groups and some joining at senior level, the new athletic year will hopefully bring even further success right across the age range.

Andrew Goudie gained his Great Britain Junior Vest this year in a match in Germany and was joined by Nigel Whorlow who received his call to represent England at the end of September. Our younger athletes also got a taste of International racing by competing for the RWA 'Young England' team vs. Ireland in Dublin; Laura Fryer (for the second time), Paul Graham and Sam McNally were the Bels representatives in this match.

For first time Race Walking was included in the Schools International Athletic Match (England vs. Ireland vs. Scotland vs. Wales). The event was held in Dublin and Nigel Whorlow represented the English Schools and gained the bronze medal. To compete in this event is the highest representative honour available in Schools Athletics.

Six of our youngsters were picked for the Southern Area in the Inter-Area Match but due to prior engagements only two of them were able to take up the invitation. This did not stop Paul Graham winning the U13 Boys event. We also had representatives in the U15, U20 and senior Inter-County matches, with Nigel gaining a U.K. All-Time Top Ten time in the 3,000m at Crawley. Our regional and county representation is strong and is likely to get even stronger over the coming year. We also had many national, regional and county champions - too many to name them all here but they are all listed in the race reports. Overall we have gained 4 county golds and 2 silvers followed by 2 regional golds, 3 silvers and 2 bronze. On the national ranking scene we have quite a few young athletes at or near the top of the lists

All looks well for the future of Race Walking within the Club. We have now established ourselves as one of the top three walking clubs in the country, both of the clubs above us being dedicated solely to walking. With the success we have been having the Race Walking section within the Club now stands proudly alongside all the other sections who have done the Bels proud over the past couple of years.

One of the problems with Race Walking is the lack of encouragement from other sections of the Club. Youngsters who want to try out various events rarely get pointed in the 'walking' direction. It must seem very strange to those outside our Club when they see that all our youngsters in the race walking section live miles from London. I am sure that we must be able to get some of our other young athletes interested in the event. We have young walkers in Kent and Suffolk but none from our home county of Surrey.

Finally thanks must go to those seniors who have helped coach our youngsters to their present high standard. They include Mabon Dane, Mick Graham, Dave Sharpe and Ray Hall.

I look forward to another successful year.

Stephen Whorlow Young Athletes Race Walking Team Manager

Andrew GoudieNigel WhorlowLaura FryerPaul GrahamSam McNallyMabon DaneMick GrahamDave SharpeRay HallSteve Whorlow Jnr.Kevin BakerLucy AslingStephen WhorlowAlan Mead

CAMBRIDGE HARRIERS WINTER LEAGUE BEXLEY, 8 MAR 1997

The final match of the League at Bexley included the SRWA YAG Champs. and again the Bels. youngsters were up for the honours. It was much the same for the Seniors, once at the forefront of Race Walking in the South.

In the YAG races young Sam McNally was able to score in both the U11 and U13 events due to the unique rules of the competition. He won the race and the overall competition in the under 11s and then went on to take 4th place in the under 13/15 race. In the girls' race Lucy Asling took 3rd in the race which also gave her 3rd overall.

The under 13/15 race was a two in one event, with under 13 only counting for the RWA Champs. Paul Graham took the Regional Championship, with Sam racing to the silver and Stephen Whorlow coming home 4th to help the Club take the team title. In the girls' under 15 event Laura Fryer continued her competition against fellow Kent athlete Nichola Phillips from Dartford - a challenge that was to last throughout the season. Laura took the silver medal.

Nigel Whorlow was in a class of his own in winning the under 17 title, leading all the way. Andrew Goudie found the going tough against the winner in the under 20 age group but still managed to earn the silver medal.

Stephen Whorlow

Up North again for the Sheffield Grand Prix, this being a rehearsal for the Young Athletes National Championships a fortnight later. A 7am start for a five hour trip does not lend itself to the best performances but the Bels. team again acquitted themselves very well. Report · page 15

MICKY BROWN OPEN & YA GRAND PRIX STEYNING, 22 JUN 1997

Stephen Whorlow

The trip to Steyning was made very difficult due to the races being held well off the beaten track. Nevertheless, with three trains and a bus journey for the Folkestone Squad and our boys from Haverhill staying overnight nearby to ensure they were there on time, the Club did very well to get the numbers out and provide the other teams with some stiff competition. It was well worth the effort as nearly everyone scored a pb. in spite of it being one of the wettest days of the year and many of the day's spoils went to the Bels. team.

Starting with the under 11 girls, Lucy Asling had not only to contend with the worst of the weather but with some of the competitors who still needed a bit more coaching in the 'niceties' of Race Walking. She managed to come home in 2nd place with a big pb. In the under 11 boys' event we had our young 7 year old Andrew Graham showing how the event should be walked and he joined Lucy as one of the best stylists in the younger age groups as well as also getting 2nd place in another pb. Newcomer Jeremy Dawson also showed that he has the stamina and courage for the event. Among the under 13 boys young Paul Graham has started to put plenty of space between himself and the rest. Again he was 1st and with Sam McNally 3rd and Stephen Whorlow 5th (with a massive pb knocking over 1½ minutes off his previous best) the winning team prize was theirs once more.

Laura Fryer's was the best race of the day. In the under 15 girls' event she chased the National Champion, Nicola Phillips of Dartford, all the way to the line. Laura had actually beaten her rival and friend the previous week in the Kent Schools Championships but here just lost out by 10 metres in spite of reducing her best ever time by another 18 seconds.

The under 17 men's 5km race went to Nigel Whorlow who learned a lesson about even paced racing. He had worked out his lap times exactly and kept to them for the whole race but couldn't understand why he had not achieved his expected time. Oh dear! He had forgotten to add on a bit for the additional 200m at the beginning, making it a costly mistake in respect of his time. Racing at the same time, but in the U20M group was Andrew Goudie who gained a very respectable 3rd place to become our first member home in a race that also included the senior men and veterans. In the senior event Mick Graham (M50) set off like a train but was caught by Carl Lawton half way through the race. Mick, now one of the country's top veterans, also finished with a best ever time. AAA U15/U17 CHAMPS. BIRMINGHAM, 17 AUG 1997

Race Walking

AAA U15/U17 CHAMPS. BIRMINGHAM, 17 AUG 1997

We were well represented in the Championships this year. As previously the under 13s had to compete as under 15s and again they acquitted themselves well. But it was in the older age groups that we took our medals. Laura Fryer fought for the whole of her 3,000m race with Dartford's Nichola Phillips and Solihull's Kelly Mann. Unfortunately her early race tussles with the Dartford girl used up her reserves and her rival from Solihull caught and passed her on the last lap. Nevertheless, Laura can be very proud of the bronze medal she took home.

In the under 17 men's event Nigel Whorlow decided that the position was more important to him than the time. As the day heated up it was decided to put water out in this 5,000m event. The Irish had sent over their big gun in the guise of David Kidd and with Tommy Taylor of Leicester also in the race it was to be a repeat of the Schools International only three weeks earlier in Dublin. Taylor blazed off but after 5 of the 12½ laps Kidd caught him and the positions stayed that way until the end, although as the Leicester man flagged Nigel began to get his sights on him until he ran out of time. Report · page 16

BELGRAVE OPEN '7' WIMBLEDON, 22 NOV 1997

The 78th edition of the Belgrave Open Seven Mile Walk resulted in a close win for the home club. Mark Easton of Surrey Walking Club stamped his authority on the race from the very outset. He was tracked by Steyning's Gareth Brown for the first half of the race but took the West Wimbledon hills in his stride as he went on to win by nearly a minute. The Sussex based club provided the second and third men home and with only four to score the team race looked to be heading Steyning's way but although out of the individual medals, the Belgrave squad packed their top competitors into 5th, 6th, 8th and 9th places to pip the Sussex men by three points - 28 points to 31.

Junior Andrew Goudie was the Bels. first man home (54:45), one place ahead of club-mate Nigel Whorlow (55:37), still only an under 17. Mabon Dane was 8th (58:30) with veteran Team Manager Carl Lawton closing in the quartet (58:49). It was a tremendous effort for Carl who had been up until 4am dismantling the scaffolding and seating erected in Covent Garden for the Children in Need appeal. With little rest he then masterminded the race - and took home a first team prize to boot.

Many of the Club's younger walking stars were also in action over the 2 kilometre course and between them they made virtually a clean sweep by winning every age group but one. Kevin Baker was the first under 17 in 10:41 but was actually pipped on the line by the outstanding Paul Graham whose 10:40 gave him the under 13 boys' title. Laura Fryer won the under 15 girls' event in 10:54 while Steve Whorlow took the boys' race in 13:12. Lucy Asling, only in her eleventh year, was 3rd in the girls' under 13 event in 14:04.

4, Surrey WC 54; 5, London Vidarians 127.

Pipped on the line! Paul Graham just manages to squeeze past Kevin Baker at the end of the Young Athletes 2k event at Wimbledon on 22nd November.

Men

Report · page 16

INTER-COUNTIES MATCH CRAWLEY, 30 AUG 1997

In a 3,000m track race with four Belgrave athletes competing for three different counties it was hard to know who to shout for, but the race did produce one of the finest inter-counties races for a long time. As far as the Bels athletes were concerned it developed into two separate races. Andrew Goudie vs. Nigel Whorlow (both Kent), and John Hall (Middx) vs. Mick Graham (Suffolk). Nigel and Andrew were side by side for the first 3 laps and then, as if something had startled him, Nigel took off and produced his race of the season. He almost passed Andy O'Rawe from Essex and produced a time that puts him in the top ten Under 17 All Time U.K. List. Andrew followed him home in 5th place while Mick decided that tagging closely onto John would give him a personal best. 4, (3rd in match) N.Whorlow 13:11.5; 5, A.Goudie 13:24.6; 7, J.Hall (M45) 14:22.8, 8 M.Graham (M45) 14:25.4.

Andrew GoudieNigel WhorlowJohn HallMick GrahamAndy O'Rawe
