Report · page 34

Jim Estall overall league winner

REEBOK SURREY C.C. LEAGUE DIV. ONE, RACE 4 PETERSHAM, 28 MAR 1998

Surprisingly the following day's Inter-Counties and British Trial didn't affect the race as much as it might have done. Hounslow certainly suffered the most but with Gary and Kassa pushing along the pace and Herne Hill's Solly and non-scoring Phersson in attendance there was still plenty of class up the front. Gary in particulary looked much more at ease after a few more weeks of training and it wasn't long before the two Belgravians forged away and then ran around together, crossing the line with linked hands held aloft to warn the judges that they did not want to be separated on this occasion.

Jim Estall certainly noted the difference in Gary between this race and the SoT two weeks earlier. He found the early pace beyond him but was then able to maintain his speed when many of the others began to fall back. The run took him up to 95 miles for the week – yet another week of high mileage – and more importantly, not only took him to the veterans title but also made him the overall individual winner. His face was a picture when it was announced that he had won the Mitchell Cup; he had not dreamed that he was in the reckoning. Only three Belgravians have ever won this trophy:- Gerry North (1968), Adrian Major (1970) and John Wright (1987).

Once again we missed our middle order runners (ill, buying houses and injured) and then we had Roger Alsop, Kevin Nash, Alex Hutchinson and Clive Gilby in action at Cardiff the following day so had to go back to the 60s to close in. It's there though and one of these days we'll pull it off.

Things are gradually looking up in the younger age groups. Not only was 18 year-old Kevin Quinn our fourth man in the senior race but Manuel Garcia ran away from the Under 17 field for his first Surrey League victory. 'Manny' is on a hot streak at the moment having won both the London Schools and the Inter-County Schools titles. Good running too from Robert Metalle in 4th.

Feature · page 36

British Athletics League Division One '97 – The Gameplan

Following two tantalisingly close and ultimately unsuccessful attempts at the League and Cup double, plans were formulated early season to make certain that 1997 would see both trophies on Belgrave's shelves.

You may recall that in 1995 we finished as League Champions and tied the Cup Final with Newham and Essex Beagles – only to lose out with fewer winners. You can't get much closer than that to gaining the double.

And 1996 was almost as tight. Sure enough we won the Cup comfortably but this time tripped up to lose the League by a mere 3 points to Sale!

This year, we decided that the League should be given top priority early season. No more late season catching up. A couple of quick wins in Matches 1 and 2 and we hoped that the opposition would roll over. Sir Eddie promised us all help, by getting our US based athletes back for the first two matches and with this promise we planned confidently for a good start and a relaxing year for the joint team managers. Fat chance!

To find out how events panned out read on!

Sir Eddie

Report · page 36

New boys get started ... MATCH 1 ~ ENFIELD, 3 MAY 1997

Bill Laws

Reports and stats. by Bill Laws

New boys get started ...

MATCH 1 ~ ENFIELD, 3 MAY 1997

At the start of the week the team score was predicted to be 361. But as time progressed, late withdrawals made this seem unlikely and our hopes fell. Key losses were potential big points scorers: John Regis, Dave Smith in the hammer and Matt Yates in the 800, all suffering from post warm weather training injuries. With Phil Spivey also carrying a shoulder injury, our customary early big points in the hammer had disappeared. 1996 points leader Kevin Brown, picked up the hammer for the first time on the previous Thursday and achieved a very creditable 40m in the match competition but nevertheless our usual 28 points were down to 9.

Then the new boys started! First Stuart Clark in the long jump and then Matt Douglas in both hurdles contributed valuable points. So too did Matt Simson, winning the 'A' shot in his first competition for Belgrave. We also welcomed Nick Dawson who gained a creditable 4th in the 'B' 200.

Added to this were returns to 'A' Team competition for Noel Levy, Jason Fergus, Marcello Bizio and not least old hand Darrell Maynard in the 'A' 800. While the injured Matt Yates gave vocal support, Darrel produced the best judged race that we had seen him run, moving from 7th at 600 with a startling change of pace to win in 1.51.8 after a 57 first lap. His reward - a picture in AW.

On target

Slowly it was dawning on us that everyone was performing above expectations and even with Ian Tullett pulling up in the pole vault with a hamstring injury without clearing a height, we were still on target for a respectable final score.

Maximum points in the discus, high jump, and shot backed up by individual wins from Darrell Maynard ('A' 800), Matt Douglas ('B' 400), Justin Chaston ('A' 3000 s/c), James Hilston ('B' 400) and Philip Idowu ('A' triple jump) moved us well clear of the opposition. David Pamah contributed good points with fine runs in both the 800 and 1500. Simon Shirley showed the value of having a multi-eventer in the team as he plugged gaps and contributed 22 points to the final total. The relays were a formality with a scratch sprint squad performing well to finish second and ahead of Shaftesbury – much to the delight of Sir Eddie who, it seems, this year has a side bet on with the Team Manager of Shaftesbury. He has wagered that our sprint relay boys will outperform Shaftesbury over the 4 matches. Well they did this time, but only by 3/100ths.

And so at the end of an exciting afternoon's competition, Matt Douglas (in his 3rd event of the day) crossed the line to anchor our long relay squad to a solid performance and to give a final points score of 363.

Item · page 36

Armless fun?

A mass tug-of-war in Taiwan went disastrously wrong when two of the 1,600 participants had their arms ripped off when the rope snapped. Chen Ming-kuo and Yang Chung-ming each wrapped the rope around their left arms to improve their grip. However, they were simply yanked from their sockets when the rope snapped seconds into the contest. Another 45 people were slightly injured. Amazingly doctors at a Taipei hospital were able to reattach the men's arms in seven hour operations. They are expected to recover around 70 per cent full usage of their arms.

Chen Ming-kuoYang Chung-ming
See it on the printed page →
Report · page 37

Missing the kids ! MATCH 2 ~ COPTHALL STADIUM, 7 JUN 97

It all started so well. Dave Smith, back from injury, muscled the hammer over 70m and new member, John Urquhart secured a good fourth place in the 'B' event to ensure better points than Match 1. Then Chris Rawlinson, keen to secure a Europa Cup place, lined up with Gary Cadogan in his sights in the 'A string 400H. Overcoming a daunting 4m/s headwind he left the opposition trailing as he clocked a lifetime best, a League record and a World qualifying time of 49.92. The ever reliable Matt Douglas coasted home to win the 'B' 400H in relaxed style. Good points in the 800 from Matt Yates and a slightly injured Darrell Maynard and we looked to be on course for a big score.

Cracks appeared

Then the cracks appeared. Thirty minutes before the long jump and still no Fred Salle. It transpired that food poisoning had suddenly laid him low. Mike Edwards stepped in at short notice while Stuart Clark had two fouls and then a disastrous third attempt to gain two points. Soon after, Jason Fergus struggled in the 100 with a hamstring tug and Marlon Dickson effectively did the same in the 'B'. No more racing for them today! This left us without a 'B' 200 runner while our 'A' specialist Nick Dawson also struggled with injury as the cold, windy weather took its toll. The sprinters were down and out and the afternoon was spent trying to find a foursome for the sprint relay squad.

Meanwhile maximum points came in the discus with Kevin Brown turning the tables on Lee Newman and top marks also in the high jump although Brendan Reilly was having problems with an achilles tendon. The 3000 steeplechase provided the unique sight of our pair finishing first and second to gain maximum points. Justin had jetted over from Houston, USA and welcomed new member and Inter-Counties Champion Lee Hurst who had flown in from Manchester, UK.

A great sight to see both these classy performers with Justin handling the windy conditions the better of the two. Good points in the high hurdles were followed by excellent 400 metre points with Dave Nolan running a season's best and Matt Douglas confirming what a great attribute he is - another young man to watch for in the future.

Matt Yates doubled up to win the 'B' 1500 and a first team performance for Stuart Faben saw him get the better of Nigel Bevan in the javelin. Kassa Tadesse ran a big pb in the 5000 despite the poor conditions and Jim Estall equalled his Match 1 time which meant a much better run. Mark Proctor of N&E pulled out one of those rare long putts which the rotational specialists seem to manage, so Matt Simson had to settle for second with Lee Newman winning the 'B'. And so we came to the relays with a lead of 16 points. By now Andrew Walcott had arrived and was drafted into both events. It needed Kevin Brown in the sprint relay and Ian Wells, who was still recovering from a broken foot (obtained while warm weather training, so no sympathy from the Team Management), to complete the squads. Two seventh spots were enough to see our lead drift away. Not the happiest of endings and we were rueing the fact that four of our brilliant youngsters were in Germany contributing to a great U20 GB team performance.

We had thought we would be strong enough without the youngsters. We weren't!

Report · page 37

An impregnable position MATCH 3 ~ SOLIHULL, 5 JUL 1997

Originally scheduled to be held at the refurbished Alexander Stadium, this match was re-directed to the Norman Green Stadium at Solihull where the throwing circles are notoriously poor. So we were looking to the track for the outstanding performances of the day. Matt Douglas and Noel Levy got us off to a good start in the 400H and immediately afterwards Tom Lerwill and Dave Pamah provided near maximum points in the 800m, so already things were looking good.

With Matt Yates chasing a World Cup place and producing a season's best to win the 1500m, plus maximum points from Justin Chaston and Lee Hurst in the steeplechase, and then further maximum points in the 5000m from Paul Evans and Kassa Tadessa, we looked invincible. Backed by solid performances in the sprints and relays, the only season's outing by Fred Salle and solid jump performances, we coasted home a comfortable 90 points clear of Birchfield to go 6 league points clear at the top of the League. We moved to the last fixture at Crystal Palace in an impregnable position.

Report · page 38

Europe here we come MATCH FOUR ~ CRYSTAL PALACE, 16 AUG 1997

We entered this last match knowing that we only had to finish 7th to be sure of winning the League. At the bottom end Blackheath were certain of relegation and Woodford Green also looked sure to repeat their yo-yo of up and down cycles and disappear into Division 2 for 12 months.

With this background and pressure on some members to compete the following day in a televised event at the same venue, it was difficult to get anything like a full strength team out. So thanks to all the stalwarts who made it on one of the hottest and stickiest days of the year. Pride of place goes to Paul Evans who front ran the 5000 to break the League record of 13.49.6 set by John Davies of TVH in 1974 by 2 seconds. Once again Kassa Tadessa ensured maximum points in the 5000.

Our only other track win was by Lee Hurst in the 'A' Steeplechase, although Tom Lerwill only lost the 800 by a vest thickness (according to the official photograph) being given the same time of 1.49.19 as the winner – Justin Swift Smith of Shaftesbury Barnet. The jumps were weaker than usual, so much so that we could not muster either a 'B' vaulter or a 'B' high jumper. The welcome return by Phillips Idowu boosted morale of the horizontal jumpers, the average age of which was 17 years. All four in the long and triple came from our Youth and Junior squads!

The throwers produced their normal solid performances and to the surprise of the Team Managers we snatched victory from the jaws of defeat to finish up winning the Match and the League by a very remarkable record margin. So into Europe in 1998!

Well done to everyone who competed through the season.

Less notice was given to Ian Wells who arrived at Crystal Palace planning a day out at the zoo with his family. Fortunately for us, Ian had his kit with him and despite limited training following a broken bone in his foot (obtained while on the dreaded warm weather training), Ian added more points to his BAL totals moving him ahead of John Regis on the all-time list!

Records · page 38

1997 Rankings By Event

Once again we have ranked our BAL team performances by event. The maximum four match points for all A + B events are 112, whereas for the sprint and long relays* the maximum points attainable in the four matches total 64.

Just to remind you, the complete scoring for a British League Match is as follows:

A String: 16,14,12,10,8,6,5,4. B Sting: 12,10,8,6,4,3,2,1.

The points totals and rankings for 1997 show quite a change from 1996. (1996 rankings in brackets.)

Total Points 1405

The considerable changes to the rankings in just 1 year show how important are the presence of strong A string competitors. The big improvements in: the Shot (Matt Simpson), 3000 Steeplechase (Justin Chaston and Lee Hurst), High Jump (Brendan Reilly), 5000 (Paul Evans), and 400H (Matt Douglas), show how big points can be accumulated by key individuals. Events which dropped down the rankings due to injury of key competitors were: Pole Vault (Ian Tullett and Mike Edwards), Hammer (Dave Smith), and Long Jump (Fred Salle).

Several leading juniors such as Phillips Idowu (TJ) were detained elsewhere for the best of reasons. But it is the sprints where the best potential remains for improvement. This is not going to be easy because the standards of the BAL Div 1 sprints are up to Grade A International level and the competition is very tough.

Records · page 39

Belgrave's All-Time British League Top 50 (1968-1997)

Two years have passed since Leo Coy updated our All-Time BAL points scorers. The updated list is tabled below. Out go: Warren Caswell (now with Woodford Green), Simon Maynard, Philip Okoro and Aham Amadi. In come; Darren Mead (48), Lewis Samuel (41), Kevin Brown (36) and Lee Newman (29). Relative newcomers Kevin and Lee have made massive contributions in their first 2 years with Belgrave and are bound to make further impressive gains next season.

Team Captain Phil Goedluck moves into all-time 2nd spot, but nobody approaches his 40 appearances for the Club in top level team competition. Stalwarts Mike Edwards and Nigel Bevan each move up 2 places to 6th and 7th respectively, while Ian Wells still grinds out the points to move up to 13th spot. Biggest jump is by Simon Shirley who moves up 24 places while Justin Chaston advances 18 places to 24th spot.

Obituary · page 39

Thanks to Robin Nicholls

In this and the last few editions of The Belgravian we have featured many photographs taken by Robin Nicholls of Basildon, including the tremendous shot on the back page of the last issue showing Richard David handing over to Marlon Dickson in a 4x100m at Battersea Park.

Sadly Robin died suddenly towards the end of 1997.

He was to be found at many track & field meetings throughout the South of England, shooting his way through reel after reel of film and then searching out Team Managers to hand over huge quantities of pictures taken the previous week. He always refused payment - suggesting that some charity of our own choice should benefit from whatever sum we thought fit.

We don't suppose that he was often thanked properly for his efforts so the least that we can do is record our debt to him in our magazine which benefitted so much from his work.

Robin NichollsRichard DavidMarlon Dickson
See it on the printed page →
Item · page 39

Another Small Improvement

As you will see above, Mike Small still shows up in the All-Time British League points list. Now he is pumping out the shot, hammer, javelin and discus as a veteran in Southern League and vet's meetings. It might just be worth keeping him on the British League Register though. At Horsham's Indoor Open meeting on March 1st he upped his best as a veteran to 13.22m for the shot and will be looking for more still when he gets outdoors in the summer.

Report · page 40

Numero Uno ! GOLD CUP FINAL, BEDFORD, 30 AUG 1997

With seven First Division clubs plus Team Solent taking part, the 1997 Cup Final promised some startling perfomances and we were not disappointed. We had our usual good start but Birchfield's new signing and former Olympic finallist , Nikulin made sure that Dave Smith had to fight for his hammer victory. Another solid performance from Matt Douglas earned him the bronze medal spot in the long hurdles and this, followed by Tom Lerwill's gun to tape victory in the 800m , moved us into numero uno position – which we were not to relinquish all afternoon.

The sprinting as usual proved of the highest quality with Roger Black lining up against our own Junior World record holder Dwain Chambers in the 100m. After several false starts Dwain gained bronze. Roger Black could only manage 6th spot but sought recompense in the 200m. There he had to settle for silver in the same time as the winner, Thomas of Woodford Green, clocking 20.82. Iwan Thomas was a close third just ahead of Andrew Walcott who was showing a late season return to form. In the 10000m Jim Estall, fresh from holiday and claiming to be unfit, had a good tussle with former record holder Julian Goater and moved our point score along nicely.

At this point a great shout from the infield announced a big pb by Kevin Brown in the discus to set a new club record, eliminating that of Lee Newman set earlier in the year. Dave Nolan's gold in the flat 400m, then Lee Hurst's silver in the steeplechase followed by wins for Matt Simson in the shot and Matt Yates in the 1500m increased our lead as Shaftesbury, Birchfield and Newham fought out the minor places. Stuart Faben chose this match to return to form in the javelin while Kassa Tadesse fought bravely for fourth spot in the 5000m. The sprint relay squad drafted in high hurdler Nick Cooper to secure a speedy second place and the long relay squad could then take it easy, knowing that our lead was unassailable. The return of James Hilston after an injury marred season was good to see and it was left to the Team Managers to make themselves scarce as 'Yatesy' and his cohorts took the Cup and sought out victims for their celebrations!

Report · page 40

20 points clear in the 'Semi' SEMI-FINAL, NEW RIVER STADIUM, HARINGEY, 27 JUL 1997

Not too bad a draw with Blackheath from BAL Division 1 and contenders Team Solent and Haringey from Div 2. A blend of youth and experience was the order of the day and we were never troubled after a fine start with Dave Smith returning from illness to gain an easy victory in the hammer. Next, junior Darren Thompson won the long jump on countback from Mark Faulkner of Team Solent. Jim Estall's silver in the 10000m moved us further ahead while Samson Oni equalled the winning high jump height and was unlucky to finish 3rd on countback.

Matt Douglas looked the class act in the 400m hurdles, coasting to a 1.5 second victory over Haringey's Lawrence Lynch. Matt Kloiber jetted down from holiday in Scotland to strengthen our middle distance squad and sat on Tony Whiteman as the pair raced through 400m in 52 and bits. It was a brave effort which failed only in the last 100m as Matt got pipped for second – but it was clear to see that a sub 1.50.0 was now a matter of time.

A marginal illegal wind cost Jason Fergus a season's best in the 100m while his sub 21.0 sec 200m concluded a good afternoon's work. Ex-Belgravian Marcus Adam showed a brilliant return to form as he won in the second fastest legal time by a Brit in 1997 – 20.44. Unfortunately for him, too late for World Cup selection.

A clean sweep in all the throws, with a remarkable 19.30 for Matt Simson in the shot, left us with a comfortable 20 points victory over Div 1 contenders Team Solent.

Report · page 41

Eight Bels. earn Athens tickets BUPA BRITISH CHAMPS. inc. WORLD CHAMPS. TRIALS BIRMINGHAM, 11-13 JUL 1997

International - Track & Field

Eight Bels. earn Athens tickets

BUPA BRITISH CHAMPS. inc. WORLD CHAMPS. TRIALS BIRMINGHAM, 11-13 JUL 1997

In a season riddled with politics during which the A.A.A. Championships seemed to get pushed to one side, the BUPA British Champs became the domestic meet of the season, incorporating as they did the trials for the World Championships.

As number one on times this season Chris Rawlinson went into the long hurdle race as favourite. He handled the additional pressure superbly and came away from the field to win with a handsome margin and take his best ever time down a few more notches in his quest to make certain of selection for the World Champs.

Another Belgravian to earn his trip to Athens in style was Brendan Reilly. Twelve months ago he was recovering from two operations on a snapped achilles tendon. Now here he was soaring over 2.28m to top second man Dalton Grant by 8cm.

Three silver medals came from our throwers. Last year's shot putt winner Matt Simson came up against an in-form Shaun Pickering and in spite of setting a season's best in the last round, found that he was unable to get on terms with the Haringey man. It was also a last round effort that took Kevin Brown into the Discus medals where an awesome Bob Weir had the beating of the field with every one of his six throws.

The World Champs. qualifying standard for the hammer was 74 metres and in spite the fact that the Newham & Essex Beagles man Paul Head held up best on this occasion and came away with a win, it would be Belgrave's Dave Smith being awarded the ticket for Athens, he being the only Briton to have achieved the standard (last season but still within the required time period).

Among the six heat winners of the 100m Darren Campbell and Dwain Chambers looked the most dominant and in the semi-finals too, they both came up again as race leaders. Desperate measures were necessary in the final where Campbell was so close to pulling off a fine win but could not survive a last ditch onslaught from Scotland's Ian Mackie who just shaded the Belgravian by 1/100th of a second. Campbell crashed to the track in his efforts to hold on, while 19 year-old Chambers almost bent double in his successful bid to outlean Shaftesbury's Jason Livingston and snatch the "bronze".

A headwind failed to stop Darren from reducing his best 200m time in the "semis", held less than one hour after the 100m final, but five previous sprints during the meeting was beginning to take its toll and he faded to 5th in the final.

Lee Hurst just missed the medals in the steeplechase while second-claimer Adrian Passey held all the aces in the 5k.

Report · page 41

WORLD JUNIOR RECORD FOR DWAIN CHAMBERS EUROPEAN JUNIOR CHAMPS., LJUBLIANA, SLOVENIA 24-27 JUL 1997

How proud we were when we learned that four of our Junior Men had earned themselves selection for the European Junior Championships. But when the news broke back home that Dwain Chambers had won the 100 metres gold in a World Junior Record time, well, frankly we were stunned. 10.06! Amazing! There have been some superb world sprinters in the last decade – Burrell, Lewis, Fredericks, Christie, Bailey, etc. – and the current crop is tremendous – Ato Boldon, Maurice Greene – but none of them have run faster than the Belgrave boy at 19 years of age. What a future. And what a task to keep one's feet on the floor in the face of the ensuing hype.

Don't let's underestimate the distance to travel yet to become as successful as a senior. All sorts of things have to be just right – mental, physical, luck, timing. But Dwain knows what it's all about and he seems to be keeping his head together nicely.

As the reigning European Champion you'd expect Dwain to qualify comfortably from the first round and this he did on the opening day of the Championships with 10.51. Round two later in the day was no problem either as he stretched his way to 10.42, the sort of time we have been accustomed to seeing from him this summer. In hindsight, however, we noted that this was into a stiffish breeze of 2.8 metres per second, so maybe we should have been looking for something a little special in the final; perhaps something to crack Jamie Henderson's 10 year-old British Record?

Friday 25th July. The final. Perhaps Dwain's biggest rival was fast starting Welshman Christian Malcolm but it was expected that our man would come on strong at around 70 metres. Wrong! With the race around 20 metres old the Belgrave Harrier was away from the field and looking a class apart with his controlled, upright, driving style. Across the line and initial disbelief hit everyone as the timing device showed 10.05. Then utter delight when the official time came through at 10.06 with the wind reading dead on the maximum allowed at 2 metres per second. A new star had been found for the World's athletic stage.

Gone was Barbadian Obadele Thompson's three year-old world mark, the European Record set in 1988 by East German Sven Matthes, the EJ Championship best set with a strong following wind in 1981, the British Junior Record, John Regis' senior club record set in Budapest in 1994 and the complete annhiliation of Marcus Adam's best Belgrave Junior time of 10.42.

There were high hopes too for our 400 metre men. Michael Parper had done everything right throughout the season, picking up all the domestic titles and inching his way down to 46.54 while representing Great Britain vs. Germany in June. Close behind him in almost every encounter had been James Hilston, familiar with every physio's couch in South London and here, unhappily only selected for the relay after a desperate fight back from a mid-season hamstring problem.

The opening round came on day one and Michael coolly strode to 47.65 looking every inch a medallist although the Spaniard Canal was still the favourite. Back the following day for the final and perhaps for the first time in the season the pressure got to Michael. For three-quarters of the race he looked good but as the final curve unwound the chance was gone and in an amazingly tight race he had to settle for a final position of 8th. Canal was the victor after all but little more than a second covered the field and less than 4/10ths the positions 4 to 8!

At least he was able to come away with a medal when the British team picked up bronzes in the 4 x 400m relay, Michael's split being 46.98. For James Hilston it was over before it started, his hamstring problem rearing up again before the meet got under way and thus ruling him out of the foursome.

Saturday saw the qualifying rounds of the Triple Jump get started and drawn in pool A, Phillips Idowu needed 15.50 to make the cut. No problem. He marked the sand at a nice neat 15.60 with his first effort and was away to rest before the final on the last day of the championships.

Guschchinkskiy – yes a Russian – led the triple jump final from round 1 with a mighty 16.78 but Phil's opening bound of 16.34, a best ever for him, put him in second place and a medal looked on. What a tremendous leap. You know it's not so long ago that distances like this won medals in senior major championships. Sadly a +3.3m wind reading meant that he was unable to remove our oldest (by far) senior club record – Derek Boosey's 16.21 from 1968.

Two no-jumps followed but still Phil remained in second spot. In round 4 the Belgravian was back on the board with 15.48 but the Rumanian Punga pulled out a 16.43 and Phil was down to bronze. Then round 5 saw Perez of Spain improve from 16.23 to 16.40 and in spite of our boy's best efforts it was to be that worst position of all – 4th.

a-d = 24th-27th. 100m: b 1, D.Chambers 10.06 (WJR). 400m: b 8, M.Parper 47.94 (ht a 47.65). TJ: d 4, P.Idowu 16.34 (16.34 +3.3, x, x, 15.48 +2.7, 15.07 +2.4, 15.69 +1.7).

Report · page 42

Relay Bronze! IAAF WORLD CHAMPIONSHIPS, ATHENS, 1-10 AUG 1997

Darren Campbell – 100m

Going into the Games in good form, Darren Campbell continued to impress. In round 1 on the first day of competition he needed a place in the first three to be certain of progressing. Not only did he come up with 3rd but he was only just outside his best ever time of 10.17. Back on the track later in the day and 2nd place or one of the four fastest losers was the target as he lined up against Montgomery of USA and world record holder Donovan Bailey. Fast times had already been thrown up as Boldon and Greene had gone to 9.87 and 9.90 in heat 1 but the Belgravian raised his game again and this time broke new ground with a 10.13. If it hadn't been for the exploits of young Mr. Chambers a few days earlier Darren would have been the new club record holder.

Darren's "final" had been and gone; in the semis he got blown away by Boldon, Ezinwa and Marsh – and now looked forward to the relay.

Round 1, 2nd Aug., 13 heats, first 3+9, heat 10 (+0.7), 3rd, 10.21. Round 2, 2nd Aug., 6 heats, first 2+4, heat 3 (+1.2), 3rd, 10.13 (pb). Semi-final 3rd Aug., first 4, semi-final 1 (-0.8), 8th, 10.37.

Matt Yates – 1500m

The controversy surrounding Matt's selection ahead of Tony Whiteman rumbles on and no doubt the fact that he failed to go beyond round 1 in these championships gave added weight to the arguments of his critics. One would have thought that a sub-3:39 should have been good enough for a place in the first five but that was always going to be difficult with El Guerrouj, Cacho and Di Napoli booking three of the available slots. Equalling his season's best would have given Matt a place among the fastest five losers but the violently fluctuating pace of a "games" 1500m rough-house made that unlikely and he actually ended up as 7th fastest loser.

Round 1, 3rd Aug., 4 heats, first 5+4, ht 4, 7th, 3:38.34.

Adrian Passey – 5000m

Having run marvellously in the trials and with a best ever in the 13:22 region, Adrian must have been desperately disappointed with his performance here, running way outside what he had hoped for. Round 1, 8th Aug., 2 heats, first 5+5, ht 2, 14th, 14:07.49.

Andy Tulloch – 110mH

Third place in his opening round gave Andy a comfortable passage to the next series of races but from here on it was going to take something far in excess of his pb of 13.52 to take him further as semi-final slots went to athletes churning out times around 13.20.

Round 1, 5th Aug., 6 heats, first 5+2, heat 2 (+0.8) 3rd, 13.69 Round 2, 5th Aug., 4 heats, first 3+4, heat 2 (+1.2) 7th, 13.63.

Chris Rawlinson – 400mH

Having come down from a best of 50.36 in 1996 to 49.92 in June and then 49.69 in July to qualify for these games, Chris Rawlinson had already done marvels but there was no way that he was in Athens to just to enjoy the scenery and he was determined to run to his current limits. By the time his race came up in heat 3 he was needing a place in the first three or sub 49.9 to have any hope of advancing. There was tough opposition to chase in the form of Matete of Zambia and the Czech Muzik and both of them went under 49 to set the fastest and third fastest times of the opening round. Chris was so close to his best ever time – what more could you ask – and although 5th, was actually among the fastest losers until heat four got on the track.

Round 1, 2nd Aug., 7 heats, first 3+3, heat 3, 5th, 49.72.

Brendan Reilly – HJ

A repeat of his tremendous leap in the trials would see Brendan in the final but things were looking a little shaky at 2.23 where he needed three attempts to stay in the hunt. Up another three centimetres and twice more bar clattered down onto the landing bed before the Belgrave man decided to cut his losses at that height and put everything he could into a solitary attempt at the required 2.28. It didn't come off. Ten men went on to clear 2.28 and two were taken into the final with 2.26.

Qualifying, 4th Aug., 2.28 or best 12, Group A, 13th, 2.23 (2.19o, 2.23xxo, 2.26xx-, 2.28x).

Dave Smith – Hammer

Only five men from the two pools achieved the standard needed to go straight into the final but after them, throws in the 75 metre range were two a penny and Dave Smith's 70.94 was good enough only for 14th in his pool. Our 22 year-old giant was learning plenty though and is sure to be a regular for Britain for a long while to come in an event where athletes continue improving right through their thirties.

Qualifying. 2nd Aug., 76.50 or best 12, Group B, 14th, 70.94 (70.94, 68.66, x). 5 beat 76.50 best 12 went to 75.28.

Darren Campbell & Dwain Chambers – 4x100m Relay

Apart from 'old man' Darren Braithwaite this was a new young British relay squad and our own Darren Campbell featured in the action all the way through to the final. World Junior Record holder Dwain Chambers was 'only' reserve for the first round but came in for the semis when Devonish had trouble with a hamstring. With the US team failing to pass the baton in the first round most onlookers rightly favoured the Canadian squad for the final although the battle for lesser medals appeared wide open. Darren's back straight run had the GB team fighting for the lead although ground gained in full flight seemed to be lost at each changeover. Rounding the final turn the Canadians had a winning advantage from Nigeria but after that our lads were right in the hunt for the bronze medal. Welshman Walker popped a hamstring as he handed on to last man Julian Golding but still the chance was there and at the end the Blackheath boy just outleaned the Cuban to take the medal on the line by 1/100th. The boys, including our own two, were delighted.

Round 1, 9th Aug., 4 heats, first 3+4, heat 3 1st, 38.74 - Campbell, Devonish, Braithwaite, Golding. Semi-final, 9th Aug., semi-final 1, 2nd, 38.25 - Chambers, Campbell, Braithwaite, Golding. Final, 10th Aug., 1st, Canada 37.86; 2nd, Nigeria 38.07; 3rd, Great Britain 38.14 - Braithwaite, Campbell, Walker, Golding.

Feature · page 43

The running man

This one will run and run. A new world six-day running record was set recently by Sri Lankan Suresh Joachim who covered 636 miles (the equivalent of four marathons a day) beating the old record of 633 miles with four hours still on the clock. Joachim survived on a diet of chocolate, cheese and apple juice and eschewed sleep for most of his attempt, getting just 10 hours of shut-eye in the six days. Rather than run at home he chose to lap a sports field in Sydney in Australia, bizarrely citing its climate as the main attraction.

Joachim clearly has a penchant for ridiculous endurance records. He holds the world record for continuous standing on one leg, an impressive 76 hours, 40 minutes, and says his next record breaking attempt will be on the 24-hour walking backwards standard. "The record is 95 miles in 24 hours, I hope to cover 99," he says confidently. This time he will fly out to Canada for the walk, but there is no suggestion that he regards it as a backward country.

Providing he does not suffer a reverse in that attempt, his next step will be the world non-stop ballroom dancing record. The current record is an astounding 215 days, which allows for a daily three-hour break for sleep. To break it would appear to be a debilitating challenge for any athlete. Not for Joachim, however. He claims that gaining the record, which he will attempt in the year 2000, will be " a piece of cake".

Suresh Joachim

Report · page 44

We hang on in Division Two and gain promotion in Division Eight - but changes lie ahead

Track & Field - Men's Southern League Divisions 2 & 8

We hang on in Division Two and gain promotion in Division Eight - but changes lie ahead

We have the numbers on our membership list to turn out three Men's Southern League teams but when it comes down to it we seem to struggle to put one good squad together. Several times throughout the summer we found our Division Two men battling for the lead with most of the match gone, only for the final scores to place us 4th or 5th because we had no runners to tackle 1500m, 5000m or 3000m steeplechase. Just take a look at the result below for match two at Ilford. A mere two points more and we would have won the meeting – and that with no men in the 800m, 5,000m and just one 'chaser. Two men jogging round for the point would have done it.

After two years of arguing the case back and forth we have now regretfully decided to drop our Division Eight team. Pretty ironic when you consider that "Eighters" actually gained promotion to Division Seven! The trouble is that it really is a distraction. Middle-distance stalwart Don Anderson, Team Managing for the "Eighters" has often been sorely missed as a points scorer in Division Two. Additionally, we cannot provide a full complement of officials for two Southern League teams and there is no doubt that some clubs love to point out that a "big club" like Belgrave is falling down on this score – never mind that there is a British League match and Thameside League on at the same time and with a National Junior League due tomorrow. Also, in today's busy world it is often a temptation to slip down to the nearest meeting to home for competition and then get back for that DIY when the "Twoers" are struggling for men in some far flung corner of the South.

So, our Division Eight team has gone. It's an experiment. If it doesn't work then we can resurrect it again in 1999. Sure we have a few athletes who will be looking around for a competition now but what we must do is make sure that all slots in Division Two are filled and that non-scoring sprints and middle distance events are held as they should be. Additionally there are the six Rosenheim League matches where we can turn out as many competitors as we like in non-scoring events while for veterans we have the Southern Counties Veterans' League.

Report · page 45

World Veterans' Championships

Several of the Bels Veteran Team travelled to Durban, South Africa, at the end of July to compete in the 12th World Veteran Championships. Over 550 race walkers (male & female) started the events in which the age groups ran from W35/M40 up to M90. The oldest competitor was a 93 year old man who competed in both the 5 & 20km. Our own Dave Sharpe and Doug Fotheringham are of course relative youngsters. Doug just missed the gold in the 5km. by a mere 3.4 secs. but took the silver comfortably by over 34 secs. in a field that started with 29 walkers and finished with 22.

Snowy Brooks continues to make his presence felt in World Veteran Decathlon circles. A bronze medal came his way in the M50 event with 6354 points. Winner was Germany's Rolfe Gees, 7713, from John Gordon of Australia, 6428.

5km. Road Walk. M40 6, D.Sharpe 27:55.54. M65 2, D.Fotheringham 27:43:42.

Item · page 46

League promotion at last for Women's team

Track & Field - Women's Southern League Division 3

League promotion at last for Women's team

Mission accomplished – promotion to Division Two! It was far from easy though, and now we'll really have our work cut out to stay up but there is one way of ensuring that we don't see Division Three again and that's aiming for Division One. It'll be a long haul but what an achievement that would be. For now though, let's get back to 1997 and offer our congratulations to Team Managers Cynthia James and Jacqui Smiter. Our thanks also to all those behind the scenes who did so much to help and of course a big "Well Done" to every member of the team.

Cynthia JamesJacqui SmiterDebbie HearnRay O'Donoghue

Report · page 46

Wet weekend! MATCH 2, CARN BRAE, CORNWALL, 17 MAY 1997

We badly needed that good start because match two was just about as far away as you can be sent in the Southern League. This was always going to be the difficult one and in the pouring rain of windswept Cornwall it was only fourth place for the Belles prior to a long coach journey home in soaking clothes. Angela Patten and Gail Richardson put up their normal solid show in the field events and there was a very nice double win from Harriet Payne in the under 15 sprints. Sabrina Scott was awesome in the 200 metres setting a new personal best.

When the results came in from around the South we found that only Enfield and Huntingdon had kept their unbeaten records while we had slipped to 8th overall – still within range of a top five position and promotion. The Women's Southern League is unusual in that every match score is added together to give the league positions, league points only coming into play if there is a tie. Just to show what can be done with a full team on home ground – Newquay & Par's 229½ points rocketed them from 21st in the league table to 5th!

Report · page 46

A winning start at home MATCH 1, BATTERSEA PARK, 26 APR 1997

With match one at home we were looking for nothing less than a win – and got it. Leading from the very first event, the 400 metre hurdles, we just got stronger and stronger. Sabrina Scott's sprinting and jumping contributed tremendously to the score (as it did in each of the following matches) and the 3,000 metres turned out to be really exciting as Sam Northey attempted to blast away from the field with two laps to go, only to get caught by the experienced Highgate runner around the last bend. There were just a few gaps in the team and that's all it took to stop us going to the very top of the league after the first round. Two clubs in the league had better scores so we had to be satisfied with 3rd overall.

Report · page 46

Ten wins from the Under 15's MATCH 3, ST. ALBANS 7 JUN 1997

We are always used to seeing Team Manager Jacqui Smiter in action but the third meeting at St. Alban's saw Cynthia James also leading by example. Huge cheers accompanied her every throw in the hammer and they were points well earned because we became match winners again. Junior International Sarah Roberts boosted the points tally in the sprints and in the younger age group Marcella Simon had a great run in the 800 metres with Rebecca Lester picking up the 'B'event – both recorded best ever times. Good sprinting too from Joy Raji who gained her first ever win in Southern League competition. In fact our under 15's really did us proud and provided no less than ten wins on the afternoon. As a team we moved back into contention for promotion with 4th place in the league table.

MATCH 5, STEVENAGE, 2 AUG 1997

It was great to see Gabby Collison back in the team and running away from good class opposition in the 3000m to score a notable win while Judy Oakley made it a double for the Belles by taking 'B' race as well. But what we gained on the swings we lost on the roundabouts and we were not so strong this time in our normally high-scoring sprints. Apart from young Joy Raji that is, who is coming on in leaps and bounds and here raced away to a marvellous 100m/200m double in the girls' events. Another young lady catching the eye was Marcella Simon, winning again over 800m.

Herne Hill Harriers turned out a surprisingly strong team for this meeting (although they were unable to provide a single official of any description) and with the home club also at full strength we found ourselves 3rd at the end of the day but nevertheless moving up to 4th overall.

Report · page 48

The match that never was MATCH 6, BATTERSEA PARK 6 SEP 1997

Track & Field

The match that never was

MATCH 6, BATTERSEA PARK 6 SEP 1997

Due back at home for the final fixture we felt that we had a great chance to show up well in a clash against a couple of other clubs that were also seeking promotion. However, with Diana, Princess of Wales', untimely death and her funeral arranged for this very date, virtually every other sporting event in the country was called off. We felt that it would be very insensitive to hold our meeting in Battersea Park – actually being used as an emergency parking area for those attending the funeral. The trouble was that with ourselves being in a promotion position already, cancelling the meeting could be seen by others as an easy way to avoid facing the other clubs in the league race.

The league organisers were particularly unfeeling and unhelpful, insisting that the meeting should go ahead. Alternatively, a couple of the clubs involved told us that they would not be coming anyway. With the BAF and SEAA unable to give a lead to the sport we took the decision ourselves to cancel the meeting. If the Women's Southern League said that we would forfeit promotion as a result then so be it – at least we would have done what we felt was right.

By the Thursday before the match, the intransigent WSL hierarchy eventually found themselves totally out of tune with the rest of the country and grudgingly decided themselves to cancel the last round of the league entirely. End of season results would stand as they were after the fifth round. We were up!

Diana, Princess of Wales

Report · page 49

It's four in a row for the "Rosenheimers"

Rosenheim League - Track & Field

It's four in a row for the "Rosenheimers"

The Rosenheim Plate came Belgrave's way for a fourth consecutive year but our winning streak came to an end after 15 matches during torrential rain at Tooting.

For match one at Battersea it was as you were in '96, '95 and '94 as our talented Juniors racked up some startling performances. Jon Squirrell was our nominated scorer in the 400m hurdles, the very first event, and his 56.4 was more than good enough to take top points but running as a non-scorer our second-claimer Ruben Tabares had already got home to clock 54.8, reducing the league record by 3 tenths. Marlon Dickson's 10.7 and James Hilston's 21.9 in the sprints were also superb in the cold conditions; unfortunately both were to suffer from injury as the season progressed.

Other wins came from Wesley Smith in the triple jump and Jamie Webb in the pole vault while Richard David cracked the 50 second mark in his first one-lap run of the year.

It was a lot closer at Tooting for match two where Herne Hill equalled our score on the track but lost seven points to us on the field where Samson Oni, Wesley Smith and veteran Mike Small each won their events. The 3,000 metres, while not giving us a win, did bring much pleasure as under-20s Amir Becirhodzic and Kevin Quinn turned in a couple of handy times.

No league records but Alan Painter decimated our own M50 best ever performance. Just one week into the age-group his sub-2:15 for the 800m reduced the previous mark by 10 seconds. Two matches gone, two wins and a two point gap over Herne Hill; the pattern for the season had been set.

Croydon is probably our least popular venue in this league and for the first time in a good while we were unable to cover every event when we failed to unearth anyone prepared to tackle ten flights of high hudles.

One man who never fails to turn out when he gets the chance is vaulter Jamie Webb. In unhelpful conditions Jamie at last managed to sail over the 4.60 mark he has been attacking for so long. Both Wesley Smith and James Hilston were event winners for the third meeting in a row – as were our relay runners.

Sutton & District and Croydon both surprised by getting up to second and third team positions and with Herne Hill coming in last, our Eastern Division lead suddenly opened right out to a 7 point margin.

Match four was enlivened by the appearance of a guesting team of Indian internationals. Junior Galley had a most uncomfortable 1500m run when he found himself being overtaken by Rosa Kutty with just over a lap to go. She was intent on setting a ground record while Junior had a battle on his hands to avoid being beaten by a woman. On this occasion there was no question of "ladies first" as Junior outkicked her down the straight.

It was an unusual match for the Bels. Not so strong as usual in the sprints we were nevertheless tremendously boosted in the other events as all-rounder Simon Shirley won shot, long jump and javelin, Gary Staines made the 3,000m his own, British League regular Richard Aspden took care of the high jump (but couldn't equal 400m man James Hilston's league high jump record) and Ruben Tabares took the 400m hurdles record down another peg to 54.2. Matt Yates tore the 800m field apart with a solo 1:50.7 and another league record came from Linda Staines in the 400m.

In spite of this onslaught our winning margin was only nine points.

Only two event winners at Sutton's ground but plenty of second places meant that we were up to fifteen wins in a row. It was getting closer all the time though as Herne Hill picked up their fourth runners-up spot and the other clubs battled for the remaining two places in the final.

Noel Levy (400m) and Jamie Webb again were our winners but Dotun Akinbogun shone in the 100m and non-scoring 400m where he and Joe Panton were neck and neck all down the finish straight. There was a nice little posse of 3k men out and we must mention Mike Small; where would we be in this league if it was not for our big veteran thrower turning out every single time.

At last it happened. We were beaten. In monsoon-like conditions there seemed to be some consideration as to whether the match should be held at all. But with Hercules Wimbledon lying in 5th place in the league and currently out of the final, they desperately wanted to get back on terms with Croydon and Sutton & District who were just ahead of them. With the track and high jump fan awash the meet went on! Our own team manager took stock of the situation and decided that being in an unbeatable position as far as a division win was concerned there was no point in risking injury to our high hurdler and high jumper. Both were thankfully soon heading for a bus-stop and civilization. With no 800m runner and our sprinter missing his 200m race in the deluge, we were never involved in the meeting.

Hercules Wimbledon romped away to a performance that took them into the final after all and gave some variety to the sports headlines in the local papers. Sutton & District were likewise delighted with their second spot – their first time in the final for a good spell. For the Bels. there were also good signs as two 16 year-olds won their events: Roy Hart, long jump, and Antony Stanley-Clarke, javelin. We look forward to many more points from them over the years.

And so to the final. Ruben Tabares set the ball rolling with his third league record of the season – 54.0 – and from there on it was a run away victory. Back from injury came Marlon Dickson for a 100m/200m double, Michael Peacock came good in the 400m and Clive Gilby made a successful track début in the two-lap race. Darren Thompson and Jamie Webb had big wins in long jump and pole vault and Antony Stanley-Clarke just missed 50 metres with the senior men's javelin.

To round off a memorable victory the sprinters then turned on a performance which stood up as the third fastest in the country for 1997.

COUNTY CHAMPIONSHIPS 10-11 MAY

Domestic Results Roundup - Track & Field

COUNTY CHAMPIONSHIPS 10-11 MAY

The County Championships were held in blustery conditions right across the country. At Surrey the gusts were so strong that the pole vault standards were blown down several times and at Basildon Jason Fergus had to battle a 5.2 metre head-wind as well as the opposition to take the 200m.

There's some good 400 metre talent in Wales these days. Darrell Maynard took advantage of Jamie Baulch going for a 100/200 double and picked up a nice little win over one lap for a change. The real 400m fireworks were at the "Surreys", however. Belgrave young guns Michael Parper and James Hilston disdainfully ignored their own junior age group and went for the senior men's 400 metres race. Michael came out on top and in spite of the atrocious conditions equalled the championship best and raced to a Euro Junior and Euro U23 Champs qualifing time, beating Tom Lerwill's U20 Club Record along the way.

Lee Newman found the form of his life at Crystal Palace, spinning the discus out to 60.48 to break John Hillier's 21 year-old Kent County record of 56.46 and top Abi Ekoku's Belgrave best by 40cm. Meanwhile, down at Crawley, what a super triple from Nick Dawson - obviously heading for a fine season.

Report · page 51

SCVAC VETERANS' T&F LEAGUE

1997 was a far cry from our first days in the SCVAC League when we carried away the Trophy as top men's Veteran T&F Team in the South and our women also made the final. We still have many veteran athletes among our numbers but now seem to be unable to get them to commit themselves to the four matches that make up this league. In common with the senior Southern League we have to face the handicap of going into every match almost completely without male middle distance runners. An additional problem has been the need to travel to Colindale and Harrow for a 6:30pm start after work – in fact no-one bothered at all for the latter fixture.

Next season should be different. Hercules Wimbledon, Herne Hill and ourselves each stated our intention not to go so far afield in future, even if it meant withdrawing from the League and forming our own competition. The result has been that our Mid-London Division will now have all four matches at Battersea which should make things considerably easier. Serpentine Runners and Met. Police are both happy with the idea while Parkside Harrow have gone into another division. We have two new Team Managers in Maureen Jones and Terry Lawton. In fact Maureen has already been busy spending the winter rounding up likely competitors. You never know, Serpentine Runners might even get a bit of competition this time round.

Item · page 51

Mike in good shape

M40 Mike Small is wintering well. At Crystal Palace at the beginning of February he sent the 7.26kg shot out to 13.07 to place 2nd in the SCVAC Indoor Champs. - better than he achieved all last summer outdoors. He's regularly been hitting 44-45 metres with the hammer in training and has bench pressed his best for 10 years - 5 times over 300 lbs.

Mike Small

SP: 1, L.Newman 17.41. 22 Loughborough, Students v Army v Police. 100m: 2, D.Nolan 11.1. 200m: 2, D.Nolan 21.7. DT: 1, L.Newman 58.30; K.Brown 54.68. 24-25 Crystal Palace, SEAA U20 Champs. This was the day that Nick Dawson got one over European Junior Champion to be Dwain Chambers! In the heats it had been the other way round as Chambers got home by 1/100th with 21.96/21.97. Both Marlon Dickson and Dwain Chambers were heat winners in the short sprint but whereas Marlon could only go a fraction faster in the final, hampered by a foot injury, Dwain cruised in for a fine win. The 400 heats were hot with James Hilston 48.54 and Michael Parper 48.97 both heading their races. Come the final and Michael again committed himself early. Hilston came back as they came out of the final turn but could not get on terms with Parper who jumped through the line in elation, missing the CBP by 1/100th. U13B 800m 1, F.Lewis 2:23.7. 18 Rugby, Cresta Turbines Open. DT: 3, L.Marar 54.38; 4, L.Newman 53.14. 21-22 Crystal Palace, SEAA Champs. There was a huge line up of Belgrave sprinters on show. Duaine Ladejo headed Dwain Chambers in the heats of the 100m, both clocking 10.7 into a 5.7m wind. John Regis won his heat and then Jason Fergus placed 2nd in his first round race. Ladejo qualified for final as a fastest loser but then didn't show up for the race in which young Chambers raised a few eyebrows with his 10.44. No shows were regular occurrences in the cold conditions. John Regis was fastest man in the 200m semi-finals but didn't fancy the final, won by our old mate Marcus Adam in 21.24. James Hilston pulled up with a hamstring niggle in Regis' semi. and Geoff Dearman was fastest 400m man in the first round but then he also left the weekend's activities at that. 100m: (-0.4) 1, D.Chambers 10.44; 2, J.Regis 10.57; 2nd round 4, H.Korjie 11.28 (-3.8); 3, J.Fergus 10.89 (-4.0) (10.8 ht -5.7); 2, D.Ladejo 10.78 (-2.2) (10.7ht -5.7); hts L.Campbell 12.1 (-2.9); J.Hilston 11.1 (-1.5); C.Edozie 11.5 (-0.7). 200m: 3, J.Fergus 21.39; 2nd round (-1.5) 1, J.Regis 21.04. hts 2, J.Hilston 21.9. 400m: hts 1, G.Dearman 47.55. 1500m: 12, M.Anderson 4:05.82. 110mH: ht (-2.5) 4, N.Cooper 15.35; 8, S.Shirley 16.23. 400mH: 1, M.Douglas 51.12 (ht 50.7); hts 3, R.Tabares 53.1. July 1997

1 Finsbury Park, N.London Open. BMC 800m: 1, M.Yates 1:49.6. 2 Portsmouth, Inter-Services. 100m: 2, D.Nolan 11.04. 400m: 1, D.Nolan 47.21. 5-6 Bedford, AAA U20 Champs. Incorporating European Junior Championship Trials. Fresh from beating seniors Regis and Braithwaite in SEAA Senior, Dwain Chambers three times beat the European Junior qualifying standard in the short sprint as he unleashed himself upon the best of British Under 20s. Michael Parper won his one-lap heat comfortably enough in 48.80 but come the final he was up against a tremendous field. No less than six men ducked under 48 seconds but Michael's unbeaten championship record was extended as he headed the lot with 47.10. Phillips Idowu led through rounds one and two in the triple jump thanks to his opening 15.25. He improved again in round three with 15.33 but Birchfield's Wallace came through into the number one spot with his 15.35. Phil was far from finished though and came back with a whacking 15.86 to seal the event in the last round. 100m: a (-2.4) 1, D.Chambers 10.41 (s-f 10.32 +1.7). 200m: b (+3.3) 4, N.Dawson 21.43; ht (+3.2) 2, D.Chambers 21.41 . 400m: b 1, M.Parper 47.10. 110mH: (+2.3) 6, S.Scott 15.07 (ht 15.20 + 2.0). 400mH: 2, R.Tabares 53.47 (ht a 53.26) TJ: a 1, P.Idowu 15.86 (+1.7). Records · page 54

Rankings 1997 - Track & Field - Men

There are a few venues and dates missing from this year's mammoth T&F ranking list... if you can fill in any of the blanks do let us know.

Figures in brackets after the athlete's name indicate National Ranking for the age group - where two figures are shown the first is the position in the Senior National Rankings.

We show superior wind assisted performances (greater than +2.0 metres per second) on a separate line after the athlete's name except where no other performance is known.

Where an athlete's best time for the season is manual but a slower electrical time is also known, then that it also shown on an additional line. Where a performance is blatantly 'windy' but a wind gauge was not used, or if the wind reading was known to be over the limit but is not known, then 'w' is shown.
